# Audit the day's two commits against the owner's own rules (2026-10-27)

Read-only. Two other sessions are editing files in this repo right now, so change nothing. Write only
`research/2026-10-26-three-bots/AUDIT_FINDINGS.md`. Use `.venv\Scripts\python.exe`.

A long day produced 13 mistakes that were caught one at a time, four review rounds between us, and then
two commits: **02cbebf** and **82d3f1f**. You reviewed pieces of this as it was built. This is the sweep
over the whole of it, with fresh eyes on the finished diff rather than on each change as it arrived.

`git show --stat 02cbebf 82d3f1f`, `git log -2 --format=%B`, and `git diff 659f080..HEAD` are your input.
`docs/HANDOFF.md`'s last section describes the state those commits leave behind.

## What to audit

1. **Does the commit message tell the truth?** Take every factual claim in both messages and check it
   against the diff. A commit that overstates what was done is worse than one that says less.
2. **The owner's standing rules, against the finished code** - name any place the two have come apart:
   - a trading day starts with its plan, and the plan refuses rather than reminds (P26, 07:00 Thai);
   - take only the side with the edge on a frame LARGER than the entry frame; unreadable means refuse;
   - fail-closed everywhere: a missing, unreadable or doubtful input stops trading, never permits it;
   - the loss breakers are deactivated by the owner's decision, so each trade's stop and the kill switch
     are the whole of what protects the account - nothing may weaken either;
   - `research/2026-10-26-three-bots/RULES_LOCKED.md` set 2 is frozen: no number in it may have moved.
3. **What did the diff change that nobody asked for?** The day included one case of exactly this: the
   global `risk.min_risk_reward` cage was lowered to let one lane through, which quietly loosened the
   live plan1 strategies until it was caught. Look for any other change whose blast radius is wider than
   its stated purpose - especially in `config.yaml` and anything under `app/risk/`.
4. **Is anything in the diff dead, unreachable, or contradicted by its own comment?** Several comments
   in this diff make claims about reachability and about what is tested. Check them.
5. **Run the suite yourself** (`.venv\Scripts\python -m pytest -q`) and report the real number, plus
   `ruff check app tests`, `mypy app`, `bandit -q -r app -c pyproject.toml`. If anything fails, that is
   the headline.

## How to answer

A section per item. For anything wrong: the exact file and line, how you proved it, and what it would
cost on the live DEMO account the engine is trading right now. Say plainly where you found nothing - a
clean verdict from you is worth something. End with the single most important thing to fix before the
next trading day, and whether the engine should keep running overnight as it is.

## Gotchas

- **Backtests:**
  - `CONFIG_FILE` picks the config; `--lane NAME` backtests one lane of it alone.
  - `MAX_ACCOUNT_DRAWDOWN_PERCENT=50` in the command's environment (`.env` sets 10).
  - `ALLOWED_SYMBOLS` for symbols outside `symbols.allowed`; without it: SYMBOL_NOT_ALLOWED.
  - `PYTHONUNBUFFERED=1` to see progress.
  - `--symbols` is comma-separated.
  - A bar touching both levels counts as the stop.
  - `--preset aggressive --risk R --balance B` for the aggressive preset (local CLI only).
  - A worktree has no `data/` or `.env`: pass `--data C:/Users/korap/taa/data/history` and copy `.env`.
- **Memory (16 GB, commit limit ~17.8 GB):**
  - At most 3-4 backtests at once; heavy replays two at a time at idle priority.
  - Never run full test suites next to the stack: on 2026-10-07 that crashed the engine (SQLite
    `disk I/O error`).
  - Run vitest files one at a time when the machine is busy (workers die with exit 3221226505).
  - `npm run build` with the stack paused.
- **Laptop sleep** drops the MT5 IPC link; three UNHANDLED_EXCEPTION trips in a day **latch** the breaker
  (manual `breaker reset` with `ENGINE_DB_URL=sqlite:///data/demo/engine-mt5.db`, owner's approval). Pause the
  stack before closing the laptop.
- **`.env` overrides `config.yaml`:** `ALLOWED_SYMBOLS` overrides `symbols.allowed`.
- **Tests that read the repo's `config.yaml`** (doctor, scanner, strategies API, catalog tests, the API samples)
  break when it changes: rerun them after every config edit.
  - Regenerate the samples with `TAA_UPDATE_API_SAMPLES=1 pytest tests/web/test_api_samples.py`.
  - A new strategy parameter changes the samples too.
- **Heartbeat schema is strict** (`extra="forbid"`): when the engine's heartbeat gains a field, restart the
  **web** first, then the engine. `demo-tasks.ps1 -Resume` starts web first.
- **After a frontend rebuild** the open PWA keeps the old version until the "new version" banner's Reload (or a
  hard refresh, or all tabs closed).
- **`ReasonCode` enum lines must stay on one line:** the frontend parity parser reads them, so keep their
  comments short.
- **Windows file writes:**
  - Bash heredocs/printf eat backslashes in Windows paths: write such files with the Write tool or a small
    Python script.
  - `Path.write_text` without `newline="\n"` writes CRLF (the repo is LF).
  - `scripts/tickets.py` may write CRLF: convert back.
- Research loaders select columns, not ORM entities: scratch replay databases keep their old schema.
- **Research before engine runs** (2026-10-15): a harness that reproduces the engine's backtest trade for trade
  (`box_harness.py` checks parity first) measures a year in minutes; run read-only research with
  `PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1`. The venv has no plotting library; the system `python` has Pillow.
- **Cloud history lags:** the DEMO cloud database gets M15/H1 bars late (an hour or more); check the last bar's
  time before reading "now" from it.
- **Net vs R:** a gold trade at the minimum lot can risk ~3x a currency trade, so net and R can disagree
  (F1 resume March: +0.157 R, net âˆ’80); judge by R.
- Asking MT5 for old ticks (`copy_ticks_range`) downloads whole months into the terminal's `Bases`; keep any
  backfill bounded (`ticks probe` has a free-disk guard).
- Don't edit Python or migration files during a full pytest run (the migration-parity test reads them).
- In Engine tests, `run(max_cycles=n)` ends with `shutdown()`: read the heartbeat during the run.
- **Frontend:**
  - Passing the typed i18next `t` as a parameter fails with TS2589: put the text in a small component that
    calls `useTranslation()` itself.
  - `z.looseObject` types keys as `string`: use `z.object` where the page indexes translation keys by field.
  - Windows is case-insensitive: don't name a module `gauge.ts` next to `Gauge.tsx`. A file exporting a
    component exports only components: keep helpers in `*Model.ts` files.
  - A new page: add it to `src/app/shell/nav.ts` and `src/app/routes.tsx`; engine queries go under
    `engineKey(id)`.
  - Secrets (engine keys, TOTP) never go through TanStack mutations or queries: call `apiPost` directly.
  - Template literals in `new RegExp(...)` drop backslashes: use `String.raw`.
  - A new strategy parameter or reason code needs TH/EN texts (`strategies.param.<name>` in `common.json`,
    `codes:*`).
  - i18next: a `count` parameter triggers plural lookups; name it `n` for plain numbers.
  - API samples must be stable: pin wall-clock stamps and random ids in `tests/web/test_api_samples.py`.
- Python: docstrings over 110 characters fail ruff E501. Long edits are safer as a small Python script.

  ("IPC send failed"); wait and re-run `doctor` before starting again.

### Four things had to be fixed before the PWA could drive it

The owner had set Aggressive 7.5 % in the PWA long ago and the engine never saw it. Four layers:
`sync.enabled` was false; `CLOUD_BASE_URL` was empty; `ENGINE_ID` did not match the registered engine;
and the web had been started with the system Python, so it could not decrypt the rotated engine secret.
All four are fixed. `sync.enabled` stays **false in `config.yaml`** (a fresh checkout and CI must not
need `CLOUD_*`); this machine turns it on with **`SYNC_ENABLED=true` in `.env`** - a new env override.

The trading profile is also **mirrored** in `advisory.preferences.trading_profile` so the engine has the
right numbers even when the pull fails. Those two must be changed together until the pairing is trusted.

`WEB_ENV=production`. The sign-in page and the step-up dialog both read `GET /api/v1/auth/options` and
hide the code field when the server says it is not needed; control actions still need the deliberate
step-up confirmation, just without a code.

# Rule set 1 (WITHDRAWN) â€” locked 2026-10-27, 17:5x Thai

Written **before** any day after 2026-10-27 has been traded or replayed. Everything below was chosen on
2023-09 .. 2026-10-24 and is now frozen. The point of writing it down is that from tomorrow the data has
not happened yet, so no choice made later can quietly improve these numbers.

Anything changed after today is a **new** rule set with its own section and its own start date. The rows
above it are never rewritten.

## The two rules

**A â€” do not enter against the hour's own momentum.**
Momentum of H1 = (H1 close âˆ’ its 50-bar mean) Ã· its 14-bar Wilder ATR, read from the last H1 bar closed
before the decision, signed so that positive means the frame leans the way this trade is going.
**Refuse the entry when that number is below âˆ’1.0.**

**B â€” the range bot does not break downward.** `range_break` takes the upward break only.

## Why each, in one line of evidence

| rule | evidence | holds its sign in both halves? |
|---|---|---|
| A | six bands of H1 momentum run âˆ’0.612 â†’ +0.328 R a trade, monotonic | yes at both extremes (âˆ’0.63/âˆ’0.60 and +0.70/+0.26) |
| B | a downward range break gives âˆ’0.810 R in bull and âˆ’0.817 R in bear â€” independent of the regime | yes |

## What the rules did on the history they were chosen on

| | trades | win | R a trade | total R | fitted / never seen |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the bots as they are | 248 | 33 % | âˆ’0.106 | âˆ’26.2 | âˆ’0.005 / âˆ’0.158 |
| A | 189 | 38 % | +0.026 | +5.0 | +0.216 / âˆ’0.058 |
| **A + B** | **171** | **39 %** | **+0.101** | **+17.2** | **+0.260 / +0.023** |

## What was considered and left out, and why

- **C â€” refuse when D1 momentum is stretched past +2 ATR our way** (+0.131 R, +0.191/+0.102). Left out as
  a veto because it drops 39 % of the trades for a gain that the thinner sample cannot carry. Kept as a
  candidate for *reducing size* rather than refusing, to be measured separately.
- **The owner's hard D1 side rule as a binary gate.** Measured and rejected on the evidence: aligning the
  side with the D1 regime is worth 0.06 R (âˆ’0.112 aligned against âˆ’0.173 counter over 220 trades), while
  the regime itself is worth 0.38 R regardless of side. The owner's own correction the same day â€” *"D1
  should not be that rigid; it is a quantity, not a gate"* â€” is what rule A implements, with the ordering
  the measurement found (H1 strongest at +0.148 correlation with R, then H4 +0.111, then D1 +0.047)
  rather than the ordering first proposed.
- **Session cells** (UP in Asia 07-09, DOWN in London 13-15 both held their sign). Left out of this lock:
  they are cells of 30 and 17 trades, and session is already half-encoded in the entry windows.

## The honest weakness of this lock

These two rules were chosen after seeing both halves of the history. The split into fitted and unseen
years limits the damage but does not remove it: the choice itself saw everything. The number of candidate
rules considered was small (five, listed above), so the selection premium should be far below the +0.45 R
measured for picking the best of 88 variants â€” but it is not zero.

**That is exactly why this file exists and is dated.** The clean test is the forward one: from
2026-10-28, every day is data that had not happened when these rules were written.

## How it will be judged

Day by day, week by week, in `JOURNAL.md`, three bots in one account, at most two open at once, each in
its own market condition â€” the owner's measure, not a per-rule trade count. Reported: the week's trades,
R, USD, the account, and every entry the rules refused with what it would have done.

# Rule set 2 â€” locked 2026-10-27, 19:xx Thai

## Why set 1 was withdrawn, within two hours of writing it

Two independent faults, both found by the developer session reviewing my code:

1. **The wrong parameters.** Every measurement ran `bots.P()` â€” the dataclass defaults â€” instead of the
   configuration the app ships. They differ in every bot, and for the range bot they differ in *kind*:
   `P()` defaults to `range_mode="fade"` (sell a sweep that closed back inside the box) while the shipped
   strategy is `"break"` (an M5 close out of the box). So rule B, "the range bot does not break
   downward", was derived from a setup we do not run. On the shipped bot, range sells are not the
   disaster that rule was built on.
2. **A one-hour lookahead.** `to_frame` indexes a bar by its OPEN; I shifted the index back an hour as
   if it were a close, so every H1/H4/D1 reading became available an hour before its bar closed, and the
   H4 and D1 buckets sat an hour out. Rule A's apparent strength was partly that lookahead.

Both are now impossible to repeat: `bots.run` refuses the bare defaults (`test_bots.py`), and a
hand-worked point-in-time test pins the open/close convention.

## The single rule

**The larger timeframe must lean the way the trade is going.**

H4 momentum = (H4 close âˆ’ its 50-bar mean) Ã· its 14-bar ATR, from the app's own ATR, read from the last
H4 bar **closed** before the decision, signed so positive means it leans the trade's way.
**Take the trade only when that number is at least 0.**

This is the owner's own rule of 2026-10-27 â€” *"every trade takes only the side with the edge on a
timeframe larger than the entry timeframe"* â€” with the frame the measurement chose. The entry is M5; H4
is larger. The threshold is zero, not a fitted number.

## What it does, on the shipped bots over 2023-09 .. 2026-10-24

| | trades | win | R a trade | total R | fitted / never seen |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the bots as shipped | 239 | 37 % | +0.102 | +24.3 | +0.226 (95) / +0.020 (144) |
| **H4 momentum behind â‰¥ 0** | **199** | **40 %** | **+0.210** | **+41.8** | **+0.214 (81) / +0.207 (118)** |
| and range buys only | 162 | 43 % | +0.223 | +36.1 | +0.307 (60) / +0.173 (102) |
| rule set 1's rule A alone (H1 â‰¥ âˆ’1) | 192 | 35 % | +0.123 | +23.6 | +0.216 (72) / +0.067 (120) |

The two halves give +0.214 and +0.207 â€” the closest agreement anything has shown in this work, and the
reason this rule is taken and the H1 one is not.

## The frame ordering, corrected

Correlation of "momentum behind the trade" with the trade's R, after the lookahead was removed:

| frame | correlation with R |
|---|---|
| **H4** | **+0.156** |
| H1 | +0.048 |
| D1 | +0.030 |

Before the fix I reported H1 strongest at +0.148. That was the lookahead. The owner proposed the order
H1 â†’ H4 â†’ D1 with D1 strongest; the measurement puts **H4 strongest and D1 weakest**, so the middle
frame carries it â€” nearer than D1, slower than the hour.

## What is left out, and why

- **Range buys only.** It adds +0.013 R overall and is better inside the fitted window (+0.307) than
  outside it (+0.173) â€” the opposite of what a durable rule looks like. The sell cells are now 15 and 14
  trades. Not taken; measured again when there are more.
- **The D1 side gate.** Aligning the side with the D1 regime is worth +0.122 against +0.041, about
  0.08 R â€” real but small, and the H4 rule already carries most of it.
- **Anything about stop width.** Widening the down bot's stop on the real path: x1.0 âˆ’0.031, x1.5 âˆ’0.092,
  x2.0 âˆ’0.080. The stop is not the problem; a winner's median adverse excursion is 0.29â€“0.41 R while a
  loser's is 1.04â€“1.09 R.

## The honest weakness, again

This rule was chosen after seeing both halves, like the last one. What is different: it is **one** rule
with a threshold of zero rather than a fitted number, it comes from the frame the owner named, and its
two halves agree to within 0.007 R. The clean test is still the forward one, and the forward test has not
started yet â€” the previous attempt to start it was withdrawn before a single day was run.

Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5 <noreply@anthropic.com>

02cbebf5f578a2d53decc9220df395b54eaae842
feat(risk): the trading day's plan is a gate, and the H4 lean decides the side

**P26 as a gate, not a note.** The owner recorded "every trading day starts with its plan" on
2026-10-25; it went into the knowledge base and into memory, and on the evening of 2026-10-27 the engine
still opened a DEMO session without one. A note cannot refuse anything. `app/risk/day_plan.py` makes the
plan a file whose presence is the permission, like the kill switch: missing, empty, whitespace-only,
a directory, unreadable or not valid UTF-8 all mean the day is unplanned and the mode gate fails. Open
positions are still managed and closable. The day runs 07:00 Thai to 07:00 Thai, so a morning plan
carries through the New York session.

**The H4 lean replaces the D1 side label** (`RULES_LOCKED.md` set 2): take the trade only when the lean
of a frame LARGER than the entry frame is at least 0 on the trade's own side. +0.102 -> +0.210 R a trade
over 2023-09..2026-10, +0.214 fitted / +0.207 never seen. `edge_lean` refuses an unreadable frame, a
non-finite mean or width, and a lean that overflowed to infinity - a tiny positive ATR can do that, and
`nan < 0` / `inf < 0` are both False, so a gate written as "refuse when negative" would have opened on
no evidence.

Also: the 1.0 floor under min_risk_reward is gone (the owner: "take the rule out") without touching the
outer cage, so only the three_bots lane's own 0.8 applies and plan1 keeps its 1.0; WEB_REQUIRE_TOTP lets
a single-user development service sign in without a code, refused when WEB_ENV=production, with the
sign-in page and the step-up dialog both asking the server through GET /api/v1/auth/options; SYNC_ENABLED
pairs this machine with the local web without turning sync on for a fresh checkout; and a malformed rule
suspension now says what it should have been instead of being swallowed.

Reviewed twice by the developer session, which found the fail-open `day_plan_ok=True` default, the
reachable `isfinite` guard, six unasserted contracts and - the one that mattered - that making every
integration fixture write a plan had left nothing proving the gate ever refuses. All fixed; two
end-to-end DEMO cases now assert zero `order_send` with no plan and orders flowing once it returns.

diff --git a/app/risk/day_plan.py b/app/risk/day_plan.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..b655038
--- /dev/null
+++ b/app/risk/day_plan.py
@@ -0,0 +1,79 @@
+"""The trading day's plan, as a gate rather than a reminder (P26).
+
+The owner, 2026-10-25: every trading day starts with the plan - the market's condition, yesterday's
+levels, the trend, the side with the edge, the turn/go-on scenarios - written and summarised before
+anything is traded. It was recorded as a principle (P26), as a knowledge-base entry and as a memory, and
+on the evening of 2026-10-27 the engine still opened a DEMO session with no plan at all. The owner:
+*"I went to the trouble of telling you to write it down as a principle."*
+
+A note cannot stop anything. So the plan is now a **file whose presence is the permission**, exactly
+like the kill switch: one ``stat`` per gate check, no tooling needed to satisfy it, and nothing to
+forget. Without today's plan the mode gate fails and no broker order is sent; positions already open are
+still managed, because refusing to manage them would be more dangerous than the missing plan.
+
+**The day is the owner's trading day, 07:00 Thai to 07:00 Thai**, not the calendar date: a plan written
+on Tuesday morning still covers Wednesday 02:00 Thai, in the middle of the New York session.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import logging
+from datetime import datetime, time, timedelta
+from pathlib import Path
+from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo
+
+TH = ZoneInfo("Asia/Bangkok")
+DAY_STARTS = time(7)  # the owner's trading day opens with the Asia window
+DEFAULT_DIR = Path("data/plans")
+READ_LIMIT = 64 * 1024  # enough to tell prose from an empty file
+
+log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
+
+
+def trading_date(now: datetime) -> str:
+    """The Thai trading day ``now`` belongs to, as ``YYYY-MM-DD``.
+
+    02:00 Thai on Wednesday is still Tuesday's trading day; 07:00 Thai starts the new one.
+    """
+    th = now.astimezone(TH)
+    return (th.date() - timedelta(days=1) if th.time() < DAY_STARTS else th.date()).isoformat()
+
+
+class DayPlan:
+    """Is today's plan written? Presence of the file is the whole answer."""
+
+    def __init__(self, directory: Path | str = DEFAULT_DIR) -> None:
+        self.directory = Path(directory)
+
+    def path(self, now: datetime) -> Path:
+        return self.directory / f"{trading_date(now)}.md"
+
+    def exists(self, now: datetime) -> bool:
+        """True when the day's plan file is there and not empty.
+
+        An empty file is not a plan: a shell redirect that failed halfway would otherwise open the gate,
+        and nor is one holding only whitespace (developer session, 2026-10-27).
+        """
+        p = self.path(now)
+        try:
+            if not p.is_file():
+                return False
+            with p.open("rb") as fh:  # bounded: a plan is prose, and a huge file must not be inhaled
+                head = fh.read(READ_LIMIT)
+            return bool(head.decode("utf-8").strip())
+        except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
+            # Anything unreadable - a vanished mount, no permission, bytes that are not text - is the
+            # same as no plan. A decode error used to escape as an exception rather than a DAY_PLAN
+            # refusal (developer session, 2026-10-27): the gate must say why it refused, not crash.
+            log.warning("cannot read the day plan at %s; treating the day as unplanned", p)
+            return False
+
+    def write(self, now: datetime, text: str) -> Path:
+        """Record the day's plan. Refuses to overwrite one: a day has one plan, added to, not replaced."""
+        p = self.path(now)
+        if self.exists(now):
+            raise FileExistsError(f"the plan for {trading_date(now)} is already written: {p}")
+        p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        p.write_bytes(text.encode("utf-8"))
+        log.info("the plan for %s is recorded (%s)", trading_date(now), p)
+        return p

diff --git a/app/strategy/edge_side.py b/app/strategy/edge_side.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..a262735
--- /dev/null
+++ b/app/strategy/edge_side.py
@@ -0,0 +1,109 @@
+"""Which side has the edge on a timeframe LARGER than the one the trade is entered on.
+
+The owner's rule, 2026-10-27: *"every trade takes only the side with the edge on a timeframe larger than
+the entry timeframe"*, and their correction the same day: *"D1 should not be that rigid - it is a
+quantity, not a gate; the more momentum there is, the further that side runs."*
+
+So the frame's lean is read as a **number**, not a label: the close's distance from its own 50-bar mean,
+in that frame's own 14-bar ATR, signed (positive = leaning up). A trade is allowed when the lean is on
+its side; the threshold is zero, not a fitted number.
+
+**The frame is H4, measured** (`research/2026-10-26-three-bots/RULES_LOCKED.md`, rule set 2; 239 trades,
+2023-09 .. 2026-10-24). Correlation of "lean behind the trade" with the trade's R: H4 +0.156, H1 +0.048,
+D1 +0.030 - the middle frame carries it, nearer than the day and slower than the hour. Requiring the H4
+lean to be on the trade's side moves the three bots from +0.102 to +0.210 R a trade, and the two halves
+of the history agree to 0.007 R (+0.214 fitted, +0.207 never seen) - the reason this rule was taken and
+an H1 one was not.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import math
+from collections.abc import Mapping
+
+import pandas as pd
+
+from app.core.enums import Side, Timeframe
+from app.indicators.volatility import atr
+
+MEAN_BARS = 50
+ATR_BARS = 14
+HOURS = {Timeframe.H1: 1, Timeframe.H4: 4, Timeframe.D1: 24}
+
+
+def resample_from_h1(h1: pd.DataFrame, hours: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
+    """Bars of ``hours`` from the lane's H1 frame, indexed by each bar's OPEN.
+
+    App frames are stamped at the bar's CLOSE, so the index is shifted back one hour first; the research
+    harness reads history stamped at the open and shifts nothing. Getting this backwards costs an hour of
+    lookahead, which is how a day of conclusions went wrong on 2026-10-27.
+    """
+    opens = h1.copy()
+    opens.index = pd.DatetimeIndex(opens.index) - pd.Timedelta(hours=1)
+    rule = f"{hours}h" if hours < 24 else "1D"
+    return opens.resample(rule).agg({"open": "first", "high": "max", "low": "min", "close": "last"}).dropna()
+
+
+def edge_lean(
+    frames: Mapping[Timeframe, pd.DataFrame],
+    at: pd.Timestamp,
+    entry: Timeframe,
+    *,
+    frame: Timeframe = Timeframe.H4,
+) -> float | None:
+    """The signed lean of ``frame``, or ``None`` when it cannot be read from a closed bar.
+
+    ``None`` never means "allowed": a caller that cannot read the frame above its entry has no edge side
+    and does not trade. ``entry`` must be smaller than ``frame`` - the rule is meaningless otherwise, and
+    a ``ValueError`` here stops it being read from the entry frame by accident.
+    """
+    if entry.seconds >= frame.seconds:
+        raise ValueError(f"the entry frame {entry.value} must be smaller than {frame.value}")
+    hours = HOURS.get(frame)
+    if hours is None:
+        raise ValueError(f"{frame.value} is not read from the H1 frame")
+
+    h1 = frames.get(Timeframe.H1)
+    if h1 is None or "close" not in h1:
+        return None
+    bars = resample_from_h1(h1, hours)
+    closed = bars[(bars.index + pd.Timedelta(hours=hours)) <= pd.Timestamp(at)]
+    if len(closed) < MEAN_BARS + ATR_BARS:
+        return None
+    mean = float(closed.close.rolling(MEAN_BARS).mean().iloc[-1])
+    width = float(atr(closed.high, closed.low, closed.close, ATR_BARS).iloc[-1])
+    if not math.isfinite(mean) or not math.isfinite(width) or width <= 0:
+        return None
+    lean = (float(closed.close.iloc[-1]) - mean) / width
+    # A non-finite lean must never reach a caller: `nan < 0` is False, so a NaN would sail through a
+    # gate written as "refuse when the lean is negative" and open a trade on no evidence at all
+    # (found by the developer session reviewing this gate while it was live, 2026-10-27).
+    # It IS reachable, though I first wrote that it was not: a finite, positive but tiny ATR (1e-320)
+    # overflows the division to infinity while passing both guards above. The developer session found
+    # that and was right (2026-10-27); the case now has its own test.
+    return lean if math.isfinite(lean) else None
+
+
+def behind(lean: float | None, side: Side) -> float | None:
+    """``lean`` seen from a trade's own side: positive means the frame leans the trade's way."""
+    if lean is None:
+        return None
+    return lean if side is Side.BUY else -lean
+
+
+def edge_side(
+    frames: Mapping[Timeframe, pd.DataFrame],
+    at: pd.Timestamp,
+    entry: Timeframe,
+    *,
+    frame: Timeframe = Timeframe.H4,
+) -> Side | None:
+    """The side ``frame`` leans towards, for callers that want one answer rather than the number.
+
+    A lean of exactly zero is on neither side and returns ``None``; the measured rule is "at least 0 on
+    the trade's own side", so a strategy that cares about that boundary uses :func:`behind` instead.
+    """
+    lean = edge_lean(frames, at, entry, frame=frame)
+    if lean is None or lean == 0:
+        return None
+    return Side.BUY if lean > 0 else Side.SELL

+"""A validated, inert vessel for research-supplied weighted trade readings."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import math
+from collections.abc import Mapping
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from typing import Literal
+
+Wants = Literal["high", "low", "true", "false"]
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Weight:
+    """A reading and the nonnegative amount it contributes to a weighted decision."""
+
+    name: str
+    weight: float
+    wants: Wants
+
+    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
+        if not self.name:
+            raise ValueError("weight name must not be empty")
+        if not math.isfinite(self.weight) or self.weight < 0:
+            raise ValueError("weight must be finite and >= 0")
+        if self.wants not in ("high", "low", "true", "false"):
+            raise ValueError(f"unsupported wants value: {self.wants!r}")
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Scale:
+    """The readings and minimum normalized score required to qualify."""
+
+    weights: tuple[Weight, ...]
+    threshold: float
+
+    def __post_init__(self) -> None:
+        if not math.isfinite(self.threshold) or not 0 <= self.threshold <= 1:
+            raise ValueError("threshold must be finite and in [0, 1]")
+        names = [reading.name for reading in self.weights]
+        if len(names) != len(set(names)):
+            raise ValueError("weight names must be unique")
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class ScoreResult:
+    """Normalized score, per-reading classifications, and threshold outcome."""
+
+    score: float
+    supported: list[str]
+    opposed: list[str]
+    missing: list[str]
+    meets_threshold: bool
+
+
+def score(readings: Mapping[str, float | bool | None], scale: Scale) -> ScoreResult:
+    """Score available readings over their readable weight only.
+
+    Numeric values must already be normalized to ``[0, 1]`` by the caller. ``high`` earns
+    ``weight * value`` and ``low`` earns ``weight * (1 - value)``; a numeric reading supports when
+    its contribution is at least half its weight, otherwise it opposes. Boolean readings earn full
+    weight only when their value matches ``true`` or ``false``. Missing values are excluded from
+    both numerator and denominator. The threshold is met at ``score >= threshold``; an empty or
+    zero-readable-weight scale cannot meet it.
+    """
+    numerator = 0.0
+    denominator = 0.0
+    supported: list[str] = []
+    opposed: list[str] = []
+    missing: list[str] = []
+
+    for item in scale.weights:
+        value = readings.get(item.name)
+        if value is None:
+            missing.append(item.name)
+            continue
+
+        if isinstance(value, bool):
+            if item.wants not in ("true", "false"):
+                raise ValueError(f"{item.name}: boolean reading requires wants='true' or 'false'")
+            agrees = value is (item.wants == "true")
+            contribution = item.weight if agrees else 0.0
+            if agrees:
+                supported.append(item.name)
+            else:
+                opposed.append(item.name)
+        else:
+            if item.wants not in ("high", "low"):
+                raise ValueError(f"{item.name}: numeric reading requires wants='high' or 'low'")
+            numeric = float(value)
+            if not math.isfinite(numeric) or not 0 <= numeric <= 1:
+                raise ValueError(f"{item.name}: numeric reading must be finite and in [0, 1]")
+            fraction = numeric if item.wants == "high" else 1.0 - numeric
+            contribution = item.weight * fraction
+            (supported if fraction >= 0.5 else opposed).append(item.name)
+        numerator += contribution
+        denominator += item.weight
+
+    total = numerator / denominator if denominator > 0 else 0.0
+    meets = denominator > 0 and total >= scale.threshold
+    return ScoreResult(total, supported, opposed, missing, meets)

 succeeded in 844ms:
   1: """The trading day's plan, as a gate rather than a reminder (P26).
   2: 
   3: The owner, 2026-10-25: every trading day starts with the plan - the market's condition, yesterday's
   4: levels, the trend, the side with the edge, the turn/go-on scenarios - written and summarised before
   5: anything is traded. It was recorded as a principle (P26), as a knowledge-base entry and as a memory, and
   6: on the evening of 2026-10-27 the engine still opened a DEMO session with no plan at all. The owner:
   7: *"I went to the trouble of telling you to write it down as a principle."*
   8: 
   9: A note cannot stop anything. So the plan is now a **file whose presence is the permission**, exactly
  10: like the kill switch: one ``stat`` per gate check, no tooling needed to satisfy it, and nothing to
  11: forget. Without today's plan the mode gate fails and no broker order is sent; positions already open are
  12: still managed, because refusing to manage them would be more dangerous than the missing plan.
  13: 
  14: **The day is the owner's trading day, 07:00 Thai to 07:00 Thai**, not the calendar date: a plan written
  15: on Tuesday morning still covers Wednesday 02:00 Thai, in the middle of the New York session.
  16: """
  17: 
  18: from __future__ import annotations
  19: 
  20: import logging
  21: from datetime import datetime, time, timedelta
  22: from pathlib import Path
  23: from zoneinfo import ZoneInfo
  24: 
  25: TH = ZoneInfo("Asia/Bangkok")
  26: DAY_STARTS = time(7)  # the owner's trading day opens with the Asia window
  27: DEFAULT_DIR = Path("data/plans")
  28: READ_LIMIT = 64 * 1024  # enough to tell prose from an empty file
  29: 
  30: log = logging.getLogger(__name__)
  31: 
  32: 
  33: def trading_date(now: datetime) -> str:
  34:     """The Thai trading day ``now`` belongs to, as ``YYYY-MM-DD``.
  35: 
  36:     02:00 Thai on Wednesday is still Tuesday's trading day; 07:00 Thai starts the new one.
  37:     """
  38:     th = now.astimezone(TH)
  39:     return (th.date() - timedelta(days=1) if th.time() < DAY_STARTS else th.date()).isoformat()
  40: 
  41: 
  42: class DayPlan:
  43:     """Is today's plan written? Presence of the file is the whole answer."""
  44: 
  45:     def __init__(self, directory: Path | str = DEFAULT_DIR) -> None:
  46:         self.directory = Path(directory)
  47: 
  48:     def path(self, now: datetime) -> Path:
  49:         return self.directory / f"{trading_date(now)}.md"
  50: 
  51:     def exists(self, now: datetime) -> bool:
  52:         """True when the day's plan file is there and not empty.
  53: 
  54:         An empty file is not a plan: a shell redirect that failed halfway would otherwise open the gate,
  55:         and nor is one holding only whitespace (developer session, 2026-10-27).
  56:         """
  57:         p = self.path(now)
  58:         try:
  59:             if not p.is_file():
  60:                 return False
  61:             with p.open("rb") as fh:  # bounded: a plan is prose, and a huge file must not be inhaled
  62:                 head = fh.read(READ_LIMIT)
  63:             return bool(head.decode("utf-8").strip())
  64:         except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
  65:             # Anything unreadable - a vanished mount, no permission, bytes that are not text - is the
  66:             # same as no plan. A decode error used to escape as an exception rather than a DAY_PLAN
  67:             # refusal (developer session, 2026-10-27): the gate must say why it refused, not crash.
  68:             log.warning("cannot read the day plan at %s; treating the day as unplanned", p)
  69:             return False
  70: 
  71:     def write(self, now: datetime, text: str) -> Path:
  72:         """Record the day's plan. Refuses to overwrite one: a day has one plan, added to, not replaced."""
  73:         p = self.path(now)
  74:         if self.exists(now):
  75:             raise FileExistsError(f"the plan for {trading_date(now)} is already written: {p}")
  76:         p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  77:         p.write_bytes(text.encode("utf-8"))
  78:         log.info("the plan for %s is recorded (%s)", trading_date(now), p)
  79:         return p

 succeeded in 1035ms:
   1: """Which side has the edge on a timeframe LARGER than the one the trade is entered on.
   2: 
   3: The owner's rule, 2026-10-27: *"every trade takes only the side with the edge on a timeframe larger than
   4: the entry timeframe"*, and their correction the same day: *"D1 should not be that rigid - it is a
   5: quantity, not a gate; the more momentum there is, the further that side runs."*
   6: 
   7: So the frame's lean is read as a **number**, not a label: the close's distance from its own 50-bar mean,
   8: in that frame's own 14-bar ATR, signed (positive = leaning up). A trade is allowed when the lean is on
   9: its side; the threshold is zero, not a fitted number.
  10: 
  11: **The frame is H4, measured** (`research/2026-10-26-three-bots/RULES_LOCKED.md`, rule set 2; 239 trades,
  12: 2023-09 .. 2026-10-24). Correlation of "lean behind the trade" with the trade's R: H4 +0.156, H1 +0.048,
  13: D1 +0.030 - the middle frame carries it, nearer than the day and slower than the hour. Requiring the H4
  14: lean to be on the trade's side moves the three bots from +0.102 to +0.210 R a trade, and the two halves
  15: of the history agree to 0.007 R (+0.214 fitted, +0.207 never seen) - the reason this rule was taken and
  16: an H1 one was not.
  17: """
  18: 
  19: from __future__ import annotations
  20: 
  21: import math
  22: from collections.abc import Mapping
  23: 
  24: import pandas as pd
  25: 
  26: from app.core.enums import Side, Timeframe
  27: from app.indicators.volatility import atr
  28: 
  29: MEAN_BARS = 50
  30: ATR_BARS = 14
  31: HOURS = {Timeframe.H1: 1, Timeframe.H4: 4, Timeframe.D1: 24}
  32: 
  33: 
  34: def resample_from_h1(h1: pd.DataFrame, hours: int) -> pd.DataFrame:
  35:     """Bars of ``hours`` from the lane's H1 frame, indexed by each bar's OPEN.
  36: 
  37:     App frames are stamped at the bar's CLOSE, so the index is shifted back one hour first; the research
  38:     harness reads history stamped at the open and shifts nothing. Getting this backwards costs an hour of
  39:     lookahead, which is how a day of conclusions went wrong on 2026-10-27.
  40:     """
  41:     opens = h1.copy()
  42:     opens.index = pd.DatetimeIndex(opens.index) - pd.Timedelta(hours=1)
  43:     rule = f"{hours}h" if hours < 24 else "1D"
  44:     return opens.resample(rule).agg({"open": "first", "high": "max", "low": "min", "close": "last"}).dropna()
  45: 
  46: 
  47: def edge_lean(
  48:     frames: Mapping[Timeframe, pd.DataFrame],
  49:     at: pd.Timestamp,
  50:     entry: Timeframe,
  51:     *,
  52:     frame: Timeframe = Timeframe.H4,
  53: ) -> float | None:
  54:     """The signed lean of ``frame``, or ``None`` when it cannot be read from a closed bar.
  55: 
  56:     ``None`` never means "allowed": a caller that cannot read the frame above its entry has no edge side
  57:     and does not trade. ``entry`` must be smaller than ``frame`` - the rule is meaningless otherwise, and
  58:     a ``ValueError`` here stops it being read from the entry frame by accident.
  59:     """
  60:     if entry.seconds >= frame.seconds:
  61:         raise ValueError(f"the entry frame {entry.value} must be smaller than {frame.value}")
  62:     hours = HOURS.get(frame)
  63:     if hours is None:
  64:         raise ValueError(f"{frame.value} is not read from the H1 frame")
  65: 
  66:     h1 = frames.get(Timeframe.H1)
  67:     if h1 is None or "close" not in h1:
  68:         return None
  69:     bars = resample_from_h1(h1, hours)
  70:     closed = bars[(bars.index + pd.Timedelta(hours=hours)) <= pd.Timestamp(at)]
  71:     if len(closed) < MEAN_BARS + ATR_BARS:
  72:         return None
  73:     mean = float(closed.close.rolling(MEAN_BARS).mean().iloc[-1])
  74:     width = float(atr(closed.high, closed.low, closed.close, ATR_BARS).iloc[-1])
  75:     if not math.isfinite(mean) or not math.isfinite(width) or width <= 0:
  76:         return None
  77:     lean = (float(closed.close.iloc[-1]) - mean) / width
  78:     # A non-finite lean must never reach a caller: `nan < 0` is False, so a NaN would sail through a
  79:     # gate written as "refuse when the lean is negative" and open a trade on no evidence at all
  80:     # (found by the developer session reviewing this gate while it was live, 2026-10-27).
  81:     # It IS reachable, though I first wrote that it was not: a finite, positive but tiny ATR (1e-320)
  82:     # overflows the division to infinity while passing both guards above. The developer session found
  83:     # that and was right (2026-10-27); the case now has its own test.
  84:     return lean if math.isfinite(lean) else None
  85: 
  86: 
  87: def behind(lean: float | None, side: Side) -> float | None:
  88:     """``lean`` seen from a trade's own side: positive means the frame leans the trade's way."""
  89:     if lean is None:
  90:         return None
  91:     return lean if side is Side.BUY else -lean
  92: 
  93: 
  94: def edge_side(
  95:     frames: Mapping[Timeframe, pd.DataFrame],
  96:     at: pd.Timestamp,
  97:     entry: Timeframe,
  98:     *,
  99:     frame: Timeframe = Timeframe.H4,
 100: ) -> Side | None:
 101:     """The side ``frame`` leans towards, for callers that want one answer rather than the number.
 102: 
 103:     A lean of exactly zero is on neither side and returns ``None``; the measured rule is "at least 0 on
 104:     the trade's own side", so a strategy that cares about that boundary uses :func:`behind` instead.
 105:     """
 106:     lean = edge_lean(frames, at, entry, frame=frame)
 107:     if lean is None or lean == 0:
 108:         return None
 109:     return Side.BUY if lean > 0 else Side.SELL

  77: 
  78: def evaluate_gate(
  79:     mode: TradingMode,
  80:     env: EnvSettings,
  81:     *,
  82:     account: AccountSnapshot | None,
  83:     terminal: TerminalSnapshot | None,
  84:     risk_config_ok: bool,

  86:     latched_breakers: Sequence[str] = (),
  87:     day_plan_ok: bool,  # P26: deliberately NO default - a forgotten argument must be a TypeError,
  88:     day_plan_detail: str = "",  # not silent permission (developer session, 2026-10-27)
  89: ) -> GateResult:
  90:     if not mode.may_send_broker_orders:
  91:         return GateResult(mode, ((GateCondition.MODE, f"{mode.value} never sends broker orders"),))
  92:     live = mode is TradingMode.LIVE
  93:     failures: list[tuple[GateCondition, str]] = []
  94: 
  95:     def need(condition: GateCondition, ok: bool, detail: str) -> None:
  96:         if not ok:
  97:             failures.append((condition, detail))
  98: 
  99:     if env.TRADING_MODE is not mode:

 101:             (GateCondition.MODE, f"TRADING_MODE is {env.TRADING_MODE.value}, gate asked for {mode.value}")
 102:         )

 114:         failures.append((GateCondition.ACCOUNT_VERIFIED, "no account or terminal snapshot"))
 115:     else:
 116:         identity = verify_identity(env, account, terminal)
 117:         need(GateCondition.ACCOUNT_VERIFIED, not identity, "; ".join(identity))
 118:         expected = c.ACCOUNT_TRADE_MODE_REAL if live else c.ACCOUNT_TRADE_MODE_DEMO
 119:         needed = c.ACCOUNT_TRADE_MODE_NAMES[expected]
 120:         need(
 121:             GateCondition.ACCOUNT_TRADE_MODE,
 122:             account.trade_mode == expected,
 123:             f"account is {account.trade_mode_name}, {mode.value} needs {needed}",
 124:         )
 125:         need(
 126:             GateCondition.ACCOUNT_PERMISSIONS,
 127:             account.trade_allowed and account.trade_expert,
 128:             "account trade_allowed / trade_expert is off (investor password or broker restriction)",
 129:         )
 130:         need(
 131:             GateCondition.TERMINAL_PERMISSIONS,
 132:             terminal.trade_allowed and not terminal.tradeapi_disabled,
 133:             "terminal Algo Trading is off or the Python API is disabled",
 134:         )
 135:     need(GateCondition.RISK_CONFIG, risk_config_ok, "risk configuration is invalid")
 136:     need(GateCondition.KILL_SWITCH, not kill_switch_active, "kill switch is active")
 137:     need(GateCondition.BREAKERS, not latched_breakers, f"latched breakers: {', '.join(latched_breakers)}")
 138:     return GateResult(mode, tuple(failures))
   1: """Effective risk limits: min(trading profile, local ``RiskConfig``) (PLAN Â§A31 safety, Â§A13/Â§A20; TAA-406).
   2: 
   3: Since rev. 5 (PLAN Â§A33, TAA-710) the engine owner's profile reaches the engine as a :class:`RiskProfileDoc`
   4: (``GET /api/v1/engine/risk-profile``); the local ``RiskConfig`` is the outer cage it can never exceed.
   5: 
   6: A trading profile comes from the cloud and is untrusted for raising risk. Every limit it shares with the local
   7: configuration is combined in the conservative direction (lower risk and loss caps, fewer positions, a higher
   8: minimum RR), so a profile can only make the owner's trading more conservative. Coherence rules of
   9: ``RiskConfig`` (risk per trade â‰¤ daily loss, â‰¤ total open risk) are restored by lowering, never by raising.
  10: """

  38: 
  39: class RiskMode(StrEnum):
  40:     """The Trading profile's preset (PLAN Â§A36): ``STANDARD`` inside ``risk:``, ``AGGRESSIVE`` inside the
  41:     opt-in high-risk cage (:class:`app.config.HighRiskCage`)."""
  42: 
  43:     STANDARD = "STANDARD"
  44:     AGGRESSIVE = "AGGRESSIVE"
  45: 
  46: 
  47: class HighRiskRefused(ValueError):
  48:     """An AGGRESSIVE profile met a standard cage: it never trades (the caller falls back to STANDARD)."""
  49: 
  50: 
  51: @dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
  52: class EntryPlanSpec:
  53:     """How the owner splits an entry (PLAN Â§A31 "à¹à¸šà¹ˆà¸‡à¹„à¸¡à¹‰"; TAA-1207): the engine's copy of the Trading
  54:     profile's ``entry_plan``, defined here because ``app.risk`` never imports ``app.advisory``."""
  55: 
  56:     mode: SplitMode = SplitMode.SINGLE
  57:     parts: int = 1
  58:     weights: WeightScheme = WeightScheme.EQUAL
  59:     spacing_atr: float = 0.5  # SCALE_IN: limit i sits i x spacing x ATR toward the stop
  60:     lot_unit: float | None = None  # lot per tap; None: the symbol's volume step
  61:     tp_r: tuple[float, ...] = ()  # SAME_PRICE: the take-profits (in R) of every part but the last
  62: 
  63:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
  64:         object.__setattr__(self, "mode", SplitMode(self.mode))
  65:         object.__setattr__(self, "weights", WeightScheme(self.weights))
  66:         object.__setattr__(self, "tp_r", tuple(float(r) for r in self.tp_r))
  67:         if not 1 <= self.parts <= MAX_PLAN_PARTS:
  68:             raise ValueError(f"an entry plan has 1 to {MAX_PLAN_PARTS} parts (got {self.parts})")
  69:         if (self.mode is SplitMode.SINGLE) != (self.parts == 1):
  70:             raise ValueError("a SINGLE plan has exactly one part, a split plan at least two")
  71:         if not (math.isfinite(self.spacing_atr) and 0.1 <= self.spacing_atr <= 3.0):
  72:             raise ValueError("spacing_atr must be within 0.1..3")
  73:         if self.lot_unit is not None and not (math.isfinite(self.lot_unit) and 0 < self.lot_unit <= 100):
  74:             raise ValueError("lot_unit must be within (0, 100]")
  75:         if self.mode is SplitMode.SAME_PRICE:
  76:             levels = list(self.tp_r)
  77:             if len(levels) < self.parts - 1:
  78:                 raise ValueError("SAME_PRICE needs one partial take-profit (in R) per part but the last")
  79:             if any(not math.isfinite(r) or r <= 0 for r in levels) or levels != sorted(set(levels)):
  80:                 raise ValueError("partial take-profits must be positive and increasing")
  81: 
  82:     @property
  83:     def split(self) -> bool:
  84:         return self.mode is not SplitMode.SINGLE
  85: 
  86:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, object]:
  87:         return {
  88:             "mode": self.mode.value,
  89:             "parts": self.parts,
  90:             "weights": self.weights.value,
  91:             "spacing_atr": self.spacing_atr,
  92:             "lot_unit": self.lot_unit,
  93:             "tp_r": list(self.tp_r),
  94:         }
  95: 
  96: 
  97: @dataclass(frozen=True, slots=True)
  98: class ProfileLimits:
  99:     """Trading-profile values that also exist in ``RiskConfig`` (``None``: the profile does not set it), plus
 100:     the owner's entry plan (TAA-1207), which the engine follows only when ``execution.entry_plans`` allows."""
 101: 
 102:     risk_per_trade_percent: float | None = None
 103:     total_open_risk_percent: float | None = None  # portfolio heat
 104:     max_open_positions: int | None = None
 105:     max_daily_loss_percent: float | None = None
 106:     min_risk_reward: float | None = None
 107:     min_lot_fallback: bool | None = None  # open the minimum lot above the budget (needs the cage's consent)
 108:     entry_plan: EntryPlanSpec | None = None
 109:     risk_mode: RiskMode = RiskMode.STANDARD  # AGGRESSIVE needs the high-risk cage (PLAN Â§A36)
 110: 
 111:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
 112:         object.__setattr__(self, "risk_mode", RiskMode(self.risk_mode))
 113:         for name in (
 114:             "risk_per_trade_percent",
 115:             "total_open_risk_percent",
 116:             "max_daily_loss_percent",
 117:             "min_risk_reward",
 118:         ):
 119:             value = getattr(self, name)
 120:             if value is not None and not (math.isfinite(value) and value > 0):
 121:                 raise ValueError(f"profile {name} must be a positive number (got {value!r})")
 122:         if self.max_open_positions is not None and self.max_open_positions < 1:
 123:             raise ValueError("profile max_open_positions must be >= 1")
 124: 
 125:     def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
 126:         """Without ``entry_plan`` when it is not set and without ``risk_mode`` when STANDARD, so profiles from
 127:         before TAA-1207 and before the aggressive preset (PLAN Â§A36) keep their versions."""
 128:         data: dict[str, Any] = {k: v for k, v in asdict(self).items() if k not in ("entry_plan", "risk_mode")}
 129:         if self.entry_plan is not None:
 130:             data["entry_plan"] = self.entry_plan.to_dict()
 131:         if self.risk_mode is RiskMode.AGGRESSIVE:
 132:             data["risk_mode"] = self.risk_mode.value
 133:         return data
 134: 
 135:     @property
 136:     def aggressive(self) -> bool:
 137:         return self.risk_mode is RiskMode.AGGRESSIVE
 138: 
 139:     def version(self) -> str:
 140:         return stable_hash(json.dumps(self.to_dict(), sort_keys=True, separators=(",", ":")), length=16)
 141:

 143: class EntryPlanDoc(BaseModel):
 144:     """The wire format of :class:`EntryPlanSpec` (bounds as ``EntryPlanPreferences``)."""
 145: 
 146:     model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", frozen=True, allow_inf_nan=False)
 147: 
 148:     mode: SplitMode = SplitMode.SINGLE
 149:     parts: int = Field(default=1, ge=1, le=MAX_PLAN_PARTS)
 150:     weights: WeightScheme = WeightScheme.EQUAL
 151:     spacing_atr: float = Field(default=0.5, ge=0.1, le=3.0)
 152:     lot_unit: float | None = Field(default=None, gt=0, le=100)
 153:     tp_r: tuple[float, ...] = Field(default=(), max_length=MAX_PLAN_PARTS)
 154: 
 155:     def spec(self) -> EntryPlanSpec:
 156:         return EntryPlanSpec(
 157:             self.mode, self.parts, self.weights, self.spacing_atr, self.lot_unit, tuple(self.tp_r)
 158:         )

 160: 
 161: class RiskProfileDoc(BaseModel):
 162:     """The wire format of the owner's limits (cloud â†’ engine). Validated again on the engine: a document the
 163:     engine refuses is never applied (fail closed to the last good one).
 164: 
 165:     ``risk_mode`` is on the wire only when AGGRESSIVE (:meth:`wire`): standard documents stay readable by
 166:     engines from before PLAN Â§A36, and those engines refuse an aggressive one (unknown field)."""
 167: 
 168:     model_config = ConfigDict(extra="forbid", frozen=True, allow_inf_nan=False)
 169: 
 170:     version: str = Field(min_length=1, max_length=64)
 171:     risk_per_trade_percent: float = Field(gt=0, le=HIGH_RISK_CEILING_RISK_PER_TRADE_PCT)
 172:     total_open_risk_percent: float = Field(gt=0, le=HIGH_RISK_CEILING_TOTAL_OPEN_RISK_PCT)
 173:     max_open_positions: int = Field(ge=1, le=20)
 174:     max_daily_loss_percent: float = Field(gt=0, le=HIGH_RISK_CEILING_DAILY_LOSS_PCT)
 175:     # owner 2026-10-27: the 1.0 floor removed here too, or a cloud profile could not carry 0.8
 176:     min_risk_reward: float = Field(gt=0, le=10)
 177:     min_lot_fallback: bool = False
 178:     entry_plan: EntryPlanDoc | None = None
 179:     risk_mode: RiskMode | None = None  # None: STANDARD
 180:     updated_at: datetime | None = None
 181: 
 182:     @model_validator(mode="after")
 183:     def _standard_ceilings(self) -> RiskProfileDoc:
 184:         if self.risk_mode is not RiskMode.AGGRESSIVE and (
 185:             self.risk_per_trade_percent > CEILING_RISK_PER_TRADE_PCT
 186:             or self.total_open_risk_percent > CEILING_TOTAL_OPEN_RISK_PCT
 187:             or self.max_daily_loss_percent > CEILING_DAILY_LOSS_PCT
 188:         ):
 189:             raise ValueError("a standard profile stays within the standard ceilings")
 190:         return self
 191: 
 192:     @classmethod
 193:     def of(cls, limits: ProfileLimits, updated_at: datetime | None = None) -> RiskProfileDoc:
 194:         data = limits.to_dict()
 195:         data["min_lot_fallback"] = bool(data.get("min_lot_fallback"))
 196:         return cls(version=limits.version(), updated_at=updated_at, **data)
 197: 
 198:     def wire(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
 199:         """The JSON body for the engine: ``risk_mode`` only when it is set (see the class notes)."""
 200:         data = self.model_dump(mode="json")
 201:         if self.risk_mode is None:
 202:             data.pop("risk_mode")
 203:         return data
 204: 
 205:     def limits(self) -> ProfileLimits:
 206:         """Raises ``ValueError`` when the entry plan is incoherent (the caller refuses the document)."""
 207:         return ProfileLimits(
 208:             risk_per_trade_percent=self.risk_per_trade_percent,
 209:             total_open_risk_percent=self.total_open_risk_percent,
 210:             max_open_positions=self.max_open_positions,
 211:             max_daily_loss_percent=self.max_daily_loss_percent,
 212:             min_risk_reward=self.min_risk_reward,
 213:             min_lot_fallback=self.min_lot_fallback,
 214:             entry_plan=None if self.entry_plan is None else self.entry_plan.spec(),
 215:             risk_mode=self.risk_mode or RiskMode.STANDARD,
 216:         )
 217: 
 218: 
 219: AGGRESSIVE_POSITIONS = 2  # the preset's open positions (the high-risk cage allows at most 3)
 220: 
 221: 
 222: def aggressive_limits(risk_percent: float, min_risk_reward: float | None = None) -> ProfileLimits:
 223:     """The aggressive preset's limits for risk r (PLAN Â§A36): r per trade, 2r total open risk and daily loss,
 224:     two open positions, the minimum lot when the budget buys less (the cage caps its risk; owner, 2026-10-14:
 225:     a 50-100 USD account must still trade). The Trading profile and the backtest share it."""
 226:     r = float(risk_percent)
 227:     return ProfileLimits(
 228:         risk_per_trade_percent=r,
 229:         total_open_risk_percent=2 * r,
 230:         max_open_positions=AGGRESSIVE_POSITIONS,
 231:         max_daily_loss_percent=2 * r,
 232:         min_risk_reward=min_risk_reward,
 233:         min_lot_fallback=True,
 234:         risk_mode=RiskMode.AGGRESSIVE,
 235:     )
 236: 
 237: 
 238: def governed(risk: RiskConfig) -> dict[str, float | int]:
 239:     """The ``RiskConfig`` values a profile can lower, keyed like :class:`ProfileLimits`."""
 240:     return {
 241:         "risk_per_trade_percent": risk.max_risk_per_trade_percent,
 242:         "total_open_risk_percent": risk.max_total_open_risk_percent,
 243:         "max_open_positions": risk.max_open_positions,
 244:         "max_daily_loss_percent": risk.max_daily_loss_percent,
 245:         "min_risk_reward": risk.min_risk_reward,
 246:     }
 247: 
 248: 
 249: def _lower(local: float, profile: float | None) -> float:
 250:     return local if profile is None else min(local, profile)
 251: 
 252: 
 253: def effective_risk(local: RiskConfig, profile: ProfileLimits | None) -> RiskConfig:
 254:     """The stricter value of each governed field. The result keeps the cage's class (a
 255:     :class:`HighRiskCage` stays one, with its own bounds). An AGGRESSIVE profile against a standard cage
 256:     raises :class:`HighRiskRefused`: it is never clamped into a standard cage's ceiling (PLAN Â§A36)."""
 257:     if profile is not None and profile.aggressive and not isinstance(local, HighRiskCage):
 258:         raise HighRiskRefused("an aggressive profile needs the high-risk cage")
 259:     if profile is None:
 260:         return local
 261:     daily = _lower(local.max_daily_loss_percent, profile.max_daily_loss_percent)
 262:     total = _lower(local.max_total_open_risk_percent, profile.total_open_risk_percent)
 263:     per_trade = min(_lower(local.max_risk_per_trade_percent, profile.risk_per_trade_percent), daily, total)
 264:     positions = local.max_open_positions
 265:     if profile.max_open_positions is not None:
 266:         positions = min(positions, profile.max_open_positions)
 267:     min_rr = (
 268:         local.min_risk_reward
 269:         if profile.min_risk_reward is None
 270:         else max(local.min_risk_reward, profile.min_risk_reward)
 271:     )
 272:     update: dict[str, float | int | bool] = {
 273:         "min_lot_fallback": local.min_lot_fallback and bool(profile.min_lot_fallback),
 274:         "max_daily_loss_percent": daily,
 275:         "max_total_open_risk_percent": total,
 276:         "max_risk_per_trade_percent": per_trade,
 277:         "max_open_positions": positions,
 278:         "min_risk_reward": min_rr,
 279:     }
 280:     # validate the combination (bounds and coherence) exactly as a local config would be
 281:     return type(local).model_validate({**local.model_dump(), **update})

   3: from __future__ import annotations
   4: 
   5: import math
   6: from collections.abc import Mapping
   7: from dataclasses import dataclass
   8: from typing import Literal
   9: 
  10: Wants = Literal["high", "low", "true", "false"]
  11: 
  12: 
  13: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  14: class Weight:
  15:     """A reading and the nonnegative amount it contributes to a weighted decision."""
  16: 
  17:     name: str
  18:     weight: float
  19:     wants: Wants
  20: 
  21:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
  22:         if not self.name:
  23:             raise ValueError("weight name must not be empty")
  24:         if not math.isfinite(self.weight) or self.weight < 0:
  25:             raise ValueError("weight must be finite and >= 0")
  26:         if self.wants not in ("high", "low", "true", "false"):
  27:             raise ValueError(f"unsupported wants value: {self.wants!r}")
  28: 
  29: 
  30: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  31: class Scale:
  32:     """The readings and minimum normalized score required to qualify."""
  33: 
  34:     weights: tuple[Weight, ...]
  35:     threshold: float
  36: 
  37:     def __post_init__(self) -> None:
  38:         if not math.isfinite(self.threshold) or not 0 <= self.threshold <= 1:
  39:             raise ValueError("threshold must be finite and in [0, 1]")
  40:         names = [reading.name for reading in self.weights]
  41:         if len(names) != len(set(names)):
  42:             raise ValueError("weight names must be unique")
  43: 
  44: 
  45: @dataclass(frozen=True)
  46: class ScoreResult:
  47:     """Normalized score, per-reading classifications, and threshold outcome."""
  48: 
  49:     score: float
  50:     supported: list[str]
  51:     opposed: list[str]
  52:     missing: list[str]
  53:     meets_threshold: bool
  54: 
  55: 
  56: def score(readings: Mapping[str, float | bool | None], scale: Scale) -> ScoreResult:
  57:     """Score available readings over their readable weight only.
  58: 
  59:     Numeric values must already be normalized to ``[0, 1]`` by the caller. ``high`` earns
  60:     ``weight * value`` and ``low`` earns ``weight * (1 - value)``; a numeric reading supports when
  61:     its contribution is at least half its weight, otherwise it opposes. Boolean readings earn full
  62:     weight only when their value matches ``true`` or ``false``. Missing values are excluded from
  63:     both numerator and denominator. The threshold is met at ``score >= threshold``; an empty or
  64:     zero-readable-weight scale cannot meet it.
  65:     """
  66:     numerator = 0.0
  67:     denominator = 0.0
  68:     supported: list[str] = []
  69:     opposed: list[str] = []
  70:     missing: list[str] = []
  71: 
  72:     for item in scale.weights:
  73:         value = readings.get(item.name)
  74:         if value is None:
  75:             missing.append(item.name)
  76:             continue
  77: 
  78:         if isinstance(value, bool):
  79:             if item.wants not in ("true", "false"):
  80:                 raise ValueError(f"{item.name}: boolean reading requires wants='true' or 'false'")
  81:             agrees = value is (item.wants == "true")
  82:             contribution = item.weight if agrees else 0.0
  83:             if agrees:
  84:                 supported.append(item.name)
  85:             else:
  86:                 opposed.append(item.name)
  87:         else:
  88:             if item.wants not in ("high", "low"):
  89:                 raise ValueError(f"{item.name}: numeric reading requires wants='high' or 'low'")
  90:             numeric = float(value)
  91:             if not math.isfinite(numeric) or not 0 <= numeric <= 1:
  92:                 raise ValueError(f"{item.name}: numeric reading must be finite and in [0, 1]")
  93:             fraction = numeric if item.wants == "high" else 1.0 - numeric
  94:             contribution = item.weight * fraction
  95:             (supported if fraction >= 0.5 else opposed).append(item.name)
  96:         numerator += contribution
  97:         denominator += item.weight
  98: 
  99:     total = numerator / denominator if denominator > 0 else 0.0
 100:     meets = denominator > 0 and total >= scale.threshold
 101:     return ScoreResult(total, supported, opposed, missing, meets)

  66: def check_watcher_is_not_ahead(day: date) -> None:
  67:     """Refuse a day that sits BEFORE the watcher's last call, instead of failing silently.
  68: 
  69:     The watcher's state carries from day to day, and every wake condition is ``minutes >= cooldown``
  70:     counted from ``last_call``. Replaying an earlier date makes that difference negative, so NOTHING
  71:     wakes the AI and the journal reads "0 trades" as if it had looked and declined. 2025-02-25 was
  72:     replayed against a state left at 2026-07-09 and produced exactly that (2026-10-27).
  73:     """
  74:     state = RUN / "store/watch_state.json"
  75:     if not state.exists():
  76:         return
  77:     last = json.loads(state.read_text(encoding="utf-8")).get("last_call")
  78:     if last and pd.Timestamp(last).date() > day:
  79:         raise SystemExit(
  80:             f"the watcher's last call is {pd.Timestamp(last):%Y-%m-%d %H:%M}, AFTER {day}: every wake "
  81:             "condition counts minutes from it and would go negative, so the AI would never be woken. "
  82:             f"Replay forwards, or clear last_call in {state} first (keep a copy)."
  83:         )

 100: def size100(equity: float, risk_frac: float, stop_usd: float) -> float:
 101:     """Lots for the 100 USD account: its risk over the stop's USD a lot, floored to 0.01, never below the 0.01
 102:     minimum (P15: the account's size never vetoes a trade); 0 only for an empty account."""
 103:     if equity <= 0 or stop_usd <= 0:
 104:         return 0.0
 105:     lots = math.floor(equity * risk_frac / (stop_usd * 100) / 0.01 + 1e-9) * 0.01
 106:     return round(max(lots, 0.01), 2)  # P15 (owner): the 0.01-lot minimum is recorded, never a veto

 def suspended(rows: dict[str, str], now: pd.Timestamp) -> dict[str, str]:
     """The owner's suspensions ``{rule id: until (ISO UTC)}`` still running at ``now``."""
-    return {k: v for k, v in rows.items() if pd.Timestamp(v) > now}
+    live: dict[str, str] = {}
+    for rule, until in rows.items():
+        try:
+            when = pd.Timestamp(until)
+        except (ValueError, TypeError) as exc:
+            # A suspension that silently does not suspend is worse than none: on 2026-10-27 a prose
+            # note was written here, every rule stayed in force, and the experiment it was meant to
+            # run looked as if it had worked and changed nothing.
+            raise ValueError(
+                f"the suspension of {rule} is {until!r}; it must be the ISO UTC time to suspend "
+                "UNTIL, for example '2026-12-31T00:00:00+00:00'"
+            ) from exc
+        if when > now:
+            live[rule] = until
+    return live

+- id: F23
+  kind: finding
+  stage: trend
+  items: [A1, A2]
+  says: "A frame's momentum is a QUANTITY, not a side label, and the MIDDLE frame carries it (owner 2026-10-27: 'D1 should not be that rigid; the more momentum there is, the further that side runs - or not?'). Momentum = the close's distance from its own 50-bar mean in that frame's own 14-bar ATR (the app's ATR), read from the last bar of that frame CLOSED before the decision, signed so positive leans the trade's way. H4 MOMENTUM BEHIND THE TRADE IS THE ONE THAT HOLDS: at or above 0 it gives +0.210 R a trade and the two halves agree to 0.007 R (+0.214 fitted, +0.207 never seen); below -1 it gives -0.660. The ordering by correlation with R is H4 +0.156, H1 +0.048, D1 +0.030 - so NOT D1 strongest as first proposed, and not H1 either: an earlier reading that made H1 strongest came from a one-hour lookahead. The further claim, that more D1 momentum makes a side run further, is not supported (D1 against MFE -0.063)."

Two standing instructions of the owner's were in memory and not applied: 'no entry on one or a few parameters - combine every component, weighted' (2026-10-23) and 'record the edge by market condition' (2026-10-25). The cause was copying the research harness's pass/fail shape into the production spec without asking whether the shape was right, and avoiding weights because weights must be fitted - while every gate threshold (0.382, 0.786, 1.2, 0.2) is a fitted number too, only hidden. So: before writing a spec, list the owner's standing instructions that touch it and say what each one forces the design to be, in the spec itself (P27, and the memory of the same name)."

+  items: [A1]
+  says: "A measurement can fail silently in three ways that all look like a clean answer, and all three happened on 2026-10-27. (1) THE WRONG PARAMETERS: a research script ran the dataclass defaults instead of the shipped set; for the range bot the default is a different SETUP (fade against the shipped break), so an afternoon of conclusions described something we do not run - the shipped bots were +0.102 R a trade, not -0.106. (2) THE WRONG CLOCK: history stamped at the bar's OPEN was shifted as if stamped at its CLOSE, giving an hour of lookahead and inverting which frame carried the edge. (3) A STATE LEFT AHEAD IN TIME: a replay of 2025-02-25 ran against a watcher whose last call was 2026-07-09, so every wake condition (minutes >= cooldown) went negative and the AI was never asked - the journal read '0 trades' as if it had looked and declined. The signal in all three was a COUNT that could not be right: a win rate that did not move with the stop width, 'AI woken 0 times' beside three written plans, 83 trades over a window that held three years. Stop at the count; do not write the sentence that fits what you expected."

 654:     def _presend(self, symbol: str, side: Side, price: float, expires_at: datetime) -> list[str]:
 655:         """Time-of-use checks right before ``order_send`` (PLAN Â§A12 write-ahead)."""
 656:         problems = []
 657:         gate = self.gate()
 658:         if gate is not None and not gate.passed:
 659:             problems.append("gate: " + ", ".join(sorted(c.value for c in gate.failed_conditions)))
 660:         if self.kill_switch.is_active():
 661:             problems.append("kill switch active")

 663:         if blocking:
 664:             problems.append("breakers: " + ", ".join(b.name.value for b in blocking))

 Day by day, week by week, in `JOURNAL.md`, three bots in one account, at most two open at once, each in
 its own market condition — the owner's measure, not a per-rule trade count. Reported: the week's trades,
 R, USD, the account, and every entry the rules refused with what it would have done.

+
+---
+
+# Rule set 2 — locked 2026-10-27, 19:xx Thai
+
+## Why set 1 was withdrawn, within two hours of writing it
+
+Two independent faults, both found by the developer session reviewing my code:
+
+1. **The wrong parameters.** Every measurement ran `bots.P()` — the dataclass defaults — instead of the
+   configuration the app ships. They differ in every bot, and for the range bot they differ in *kind*:
+   `P()` defaults to `range_mode="fade"` (sell a sweep that closed back inside the box) while the shipped
+   strategy is `"break"` (an M5 close out of the box). So rule B, "the range bot does not break
+   downward", was derived from a setup we do not run. On the shipped bot, range sells are not the
+   disaster that rule was built on.
+2. **A one-hour lookahead.** `to_frame` indexes a bar by its OPEN; I shifted the index back an hour as
+   if it were a close, so every H1/H4/D1 reading became available an hour before its bar closed, and the
+   H4 and D1 buckets sat an hour out. Rule A's apparent strength was partly that lookahead.
+
+Both are now impossible to repeat: `bots.run` refuses the bare defaults (`test_bots.py`), and a
+hand-worked point-in-time test pins the open/close convention.
+
+## The single rule
+
+**The larger timeframe must lean the way the trade is going.**
+
+H4 momentum = (H4 close − its 50-bar mean) ÷ its 14-bar ATR, from the app's own ATR, read from the last
+H4 bar **closed** before the decision, signed so positive means it leans the trade's way.
+**Take the trade only when that number is at least 0.**
+
+This is the owner's own rule of 2026-10-27 — *"every trade takes only the side with the edge on a
+timeframe larger than the entry timeframe"* — with the frame the measurement chose. The entry is M5; H4
+is larger. The threshold is zero, not a fitted number.
+
+## What it does, on the shipped bots over 2023-09 .. 2026-10-24
+
+| | trades | win | R a trade | total R | fitted / never seen |
+|---|---|---|---|---|---|
+| the bots as shipped | 239 | 37 % | +0.102 | +24.3 | +0.226 (95) / +0.020 (144) |
+| **H4 momentum behind ≥ 0** | **199** | **40 %** | **+0.210** | **+41.8** | **+0.214 (81) / +0.207 (118)** |
+| and range buys only | 162 | 43 % | +0.223 | +36.1 | +0.307 (60) / +0.173 (102) |
+| rule set 1's rule A alone (H1 ≥ −1) | 192 | 35 % | +0.123 | +23.6 | +0.216 (72) / +0.067 (120) |
+
+The two halves give +0.214 and +0.207 — the closest agreement anything has shown in this work, and the
+reason this rule is taken and the H1 one is not.
+
+## The frame ordering, corrected
+
+Correlation of "momentum behind the trade" with the trade's R, after the lookahead was removed:
+
+| frame | correlation with R |
+|---|---|
+| **H4** | **+0.156** |
+| H1 | +0.048 |
+| D1 | +0.030 |
+
+Before the fix I reported H1 strongest at +0.148. That was the lookahead. The owner proposed the order
+H1 → H4 → D1 with D1 strongest; the measurement puts **H4 strongest and D1 weakest**, so the middle
+frame carries it — nearer than D1, slower than the hour.
+
+## What is left out, and why
+
+- **Range buys only.** It adds +0.013 R overall and is better inside the fitted window (+0.307) than
+  outside it (+0.173) — the opposite of what a durable rule looks like. The sell cells are now 15 and 14
+  trades. Not taken; measured again when there are more.
+- **The D1 side gate.** Aligning the side with the D1 regime is worth +0.122 against +0.041, about
+  0.08 R — real but small, and the H4 rule already carries most of it.
+- **Anything about stop width.** Widening the down bot's stop on the real path: x1.0 −0.031, x1.5 −0.092,
+  x2.0 −0.080. The stop is not the problem; a winner's median adverse excursion is 0.29–0.41 R while a
+  loser's is 1.04–1.09 R.
+
+## The honest weakness, again
+
+This rule was chosen after seeing both halves, like the last one. What is different: it is **one** rule
+with a threshold of zero rather than a fitted number, it comes from the frame the owner named, and its
+two halves agree to within 0.007 R. The clean test is still the forward one, and the forward test has not
+started yet — the previous attempt to start it was withdrawn before a single day was run.

 succeeded in 0ms:
# Rules locked before the forward test — 2026-10-27, 17:5x Thai

Written **before** any day after 2026-10-27 has been traded or replayed. Everything below was chosen on
2023-09 .. 2026-10-24 and is now frozen. The point of writing it down is that from tomorrow the data has
not happened yet, so no choice made later can quietly improve these numbers.

Anything changed after today is a **new** rule set with its own section and its own start date. The rows
above it are never rewritten.

## The two rules

**A — do not enter against the hour's own momentum.**
Momentum of H1 = (H1 close − its 50-bar mean) ÷ its 14-bar Wilder ATR, read from the last H1 bar closed
before the decision, signed so that positive means the frame leans the way this trade is going.
**Refuse the entry when that number is below −1.0.**

**B — the range bot does not break downward.** `range_break` takes the upward break only.

## Why each, in one line of evidence

| rule | evidence | holds its sign in both halves? |
|---|---|---|
| A | six bands of H1 momentum run −0.612 → +0.328 R a trade, monotonic | yes at both extremes (−0.63/−0.60 and +0.70/+0.26) |
| B | a downward range break gives −0.810 R in bull and −0.817 R in bear — independent of the regime | yes |

## What the rules did on the history they were chosen on

| | trades | win | R a trade | total R | fitted / never seen |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| the bots as they are | 248 | 33 % | −0.106 | −26.2 | −0.005 / −0.158 |
| A | 189 | 38 % | +0.026 | +5.0 | +0.216 / −0.058 |
| **A + B** | **171** | **39 %** | **+0.101** | **+17.2** | **+0.260 / +0.023** |

## What was considered and left out, and why

- **C — refuse when D1 momentum is stretched past +2 ATR our way** (+0.131 R, +0.191/+0.102). Left out as
  a veto because it drops 39 % of the trades for a gain that the thinner sample cannot carry. Kept as a
  candidate for *reducing size* rather than refusing, to be measured separately.
- **The owner's hard D1 side rule as a binary gate.** Measured and rejected on the evidence: aligning the
  side with the D1 regime is worth 0.06 R (−0.112 aligned against −0.173 counter over 220 trades), while
  the regime itself is worth 0.38 R regardless of side. The owner's own correction the same day — *"D1
  should not be that rigid; it is a quantity, not a gate"* — is what rule A implements, with the ordering
  the measurement found (H1 strongest at +0.148 correlation with R, then H4 +0.111, then D1 +0.047)
  rather than the ordering first proposed.
- **Session cells** (UP in Asia 07-09, DOWN in London 13-15 both held their sign). Left out of this lock:
  they are cells of 30 and 17 trades, and session is already half-encoded in the entry windows.

## The honest weakness of this lock

These two rules were chosen after seeing both halves of the history. The split into fitted and unseen
years limits the damage but does not remove it: the choice itself saw everything. The number of candidate
rules considered was small (five, listed above), so the selection premium should be far below the +0.45 R
measured for picking the best of 88 variants — but it is not zero.

**That is exactly why this file exists and is dated.** The clean test is the forward one: from
2026-10-28, every day is data that had not happened when these rules were written.

## How it will be judged

Day by day, week by week, in `JOURNAL.md`, three bots in one account, at most two open at once, each in
its own market condition — the owner's measure, not a per-rule trade count. Reported: the week's trades,
R, USD, the account, and every entry the rules refused with what it would have done.

 100: def test_a_reading_is_never_taken_from_a_bar_that_has_not_closed() -> None:
 101:     """The mistake the developer session caught: to_frame indexes a bar by its OPEN, and an extra shift
 102:     back made every reading available an hour early and put the buckets an hour out. Hand-worked: a bar
 103:     that OPENS at 00:00 on a 4-hour frame covers 00:00-04:00, so it is readable at 04:00, not at 03:00."""
 104:     import edge_by_condition as ec
 105:     import momentum as mom
 106: 
 107:     n = 1400  # enough H1 bars for a 50-bucket mean on D1
 108:     idx = pd.date_range("2026-01-01", periods=n, freq="1h", tz="UTC")  # bar i OPENS at idx[i]
 109:     price = pd.Series(range(n), dtype=float) / 10 + 100
 110:     h1 = pd.DataFrame(
 111:         {"open": price.values, "high": price.values + 1, "low": price.values - 1, "close": price.values},
 112:         index=idx,
 113:     )
 114:     h4 = mom.momentum(h1, 4)
 115:     assert len(h4) and (h4.index.hour % 4 == 0).all()  # buckets start on the 4-hour boundary
 116:     assert (h4.readable == h4.index + pd.Timedelta(hours=4)).all()  # readable only once closed
 117:     d1 = ec.regimes(h1)
 118:     assert len(d1) and (d1.index.hour == 0).all()
 119:     assert (d1.closed_at == d1.index + pd.Timedelta(days=1)).all()
 120:     # and the reader never hands back a bar whose close is still ahead of the asking time
 121:     at = pd.Timestamp("2026-01-10 05:30", tz="UTC")
 122:     usable = h4[h4.readable <= at]
 123:     assert usable.index[-1] + pd.Timedelta(hours=4) <= at
 124:

 131:     labels = set(ec.regimes(flat).regime.iloc[55:])  # past the 50-day warmup
 132:     assert labels == {"mixed"}, labels
