 216:         - name: trend_box_up
 217:           enabled: true
 218:           # The H4 lean must be on the trade's side (RULES_LOCKED.md rule set 2, 2026-10-27):
 219:           # +0.102 -> +0.210 R a trade over 2023-09..2026-10, +0.214 fitted / +0.207 never seen.
 220:           params: {require_edge_side: true}
 221:         - name: range_break
 222:           enabled: true
 223:           # The H4 lean must be on the trade's side (RULES_LOCKED.md rule set 2, 2026-10-27):
 224:           # +0.102 -> +0.210 R a trade over 2023-09..2026-10, +0.214 fitted / +0.207 never seen.
 225:           params: {require_edge_side: true}
 226:     min_risk_reward: 0.8
 227:     management:
 228:       trend_box_down: {break_even: false, trailing: false}
  43:     rule = f"{hours}h" if hours < 24 else "1D"
  44:     return opens.resample(rule).agg({"open": "first", "high": "max", "low": "min", "close": "last"}).dropna()
  45: 
  46: 
  47: def edge_lean(
  48:     frames: Mapping[Timeframe, pd.DataFrame],
  49:     at: pd.Timestamp,
  50:     entry: Timeframe,
  51:     *,
  52:     frame: Timeframe = Timeframe.H4,
  53: ) -> float | None:
  54:     """The signed lean of ``frame``, or ``None`` when it cannot be read from a closed bar.
  55: 
  56:     ``None`` never means "allowed": a caller that cannot read the frame above its entry has no edge side
  57:     and does not trade. ``entry`` must be smaller than ``frame`` - the rule is meaningless otherwise, and
  58:     a ``ValueError`` here stops it being read from the entry frame by accident.
  59:     """
  60:     if entry.seconds >= frame.seconds:
  61:         raise ValueError(f"the entry frame {entry.value} must be smaller than {frame.value}")
  62:     hours = HOURS.get(frame)
  63:     if hours is None:
  64:         raise ValueError(f"{frame.value} is not read from the H1 frame")
  65: 
  66:     h1 = frames.get(Timeframe.H1)
  67:     if h1 is None or "close" not in h1:
  68:         return None
  69:     bars = resample_from_h1(h1, hours)
  70:     closed = bars[(bars.index + pd.Timedelta(hours=hours)) <= pd.Timestamp(at)]
  71:     if len(closed) < MEAN_BARS + ATR_BARS:
  72:         return None
  73:     mean = float(closed.close.rolling(MEAN_BARS).mean().iloc[-1])
  74:     width = float(atr(closed.high, closed.low, closed.close, ATR_BARS).iloc[-1])
  75:     if not math.isfinite(mean) or not math.isfinite(width) or width <= 0:
  76:         return None
  77:     lean = (float(closed.close.iloc[-1]) - mean) / width
  78:     # A non-finite lean must never reach a caller: `nan < 0` is False, so a NaN would sail through a
  79:     # gate written as "refuse when the lean is negative" and open a trade on no evidence at all
  80:     # (found by the developer session reviewing this gate while it was live, 2026-10-27).
  81:     # It IS reachable, though I first wrote that it was not: a finite, positive but tiny ATR (1e-320)
  82:     # overflows the division to infinity while passing both guards above. The developer session found
  83:     # that and was right (2026-10-27); the case now has its own test.
  84:     return lean if math.isfinite(lean) else None
  85: 
  86: 
  87: def behind(lean: float | None, side: Side) -> float | None:
  88:     """``lean`` seen from a trade's own side: positive means the frame leans the trade's way."""
  89:     if lean is None:
  90:         return None
  91:     return lean if side is Side.BUY else -lean
  92: 
  93: 
  94: def edge_side(
  95:     frames: Mapping[Timeframe, pd.DataFrame],
  96:     at: pd.Timestamp,
  97:     entry: Timeframe,
  98:     *,
  99:     frame: Timeframe = Timeframe.H4,
 100: ) -> Side | None:
 101:     """The side ``frame`` leans towards, for callers that want one answer rather than the number.
 102: 
 103:     A lean of exactly zero is on neither side and returns ``None``; the measured rule is "at least 0 on
 104:     the trade's own side", so a strategy that cares about that boundary uses :func:`behind` instead.
 105:     """
 106:     lean = edge_lean(frames, at, entry, frame=frame)
 107:     if lean is None or lean == 0:
 108:         return None
 109:     return Side.BUY if lean > 0 else Side.SELL

  49:         return self.directory / f"{trading_date(now)}.md"
  50: 
  51:     def exists(self, now: datetime) -> bool:
  52:         """True when the day's plan file is there and not empty.
  53: 
  54:         An empty file is not a plan: a shell redirect that failed halfway would otherwise open the gate,
  55:         and nor is one holding only whitespace (developer session, 2026-10-27).
  56:         """
  57:         p = self.path(now)
  58:         try:
  59:             if not p.is_file():
  60:                 return False
  61:             with p.open("rb") as fh:  # bounded: a plan is prose, and a huge file must not be inhaled
  62:                 head = fh.read(READ_LIMIT)
  63:             return bool(head.decode("utf-8").strip())
  64:         except (OSError, UnicodeDecodeError):
  65:             # Anything unreadable - a vanished mount, no permission, bytes that are not text - is the
  66:             # same as no plan. A decode error used to escape as an exception rather than a DAY_PLAN
  67:             # refusal (developer session, 2026-10-27): the gate must say why it refused, not crash.
  68:             log.warning("cannot read the day plan at %s; treating the day as unplanned", p)
  69:             return False
  70: 
  71:     def write(self, now: datetime, text: str) -> Path:
  72:         """Record the day's plan. Refuses to overwrite one: a day has one plan, added to, not replaced."""
  73:         p = self.path(now)
  74:         if self.exists(now):
  75:             raise FileExistsError(f"the plan for {trading_date(now)} is already written: {p}")
  76:         p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
  77:         p.write_bytes(text.encode("utf-8"))
  78:         log.info("the plan for %s is recorded (%s)", trading_date(now), p)
  79:         return p

 356:         return ProfileLimits(
 357:             risk_per_trade_percent=self.risk_per_signal_percent,
 358:             total_open_risk_percent=self.portfolio_heat_percent,
 359:             max_open_positions=self.max_positions,
 360:             max_daily_loss_percent=self.max_daily_loss_percent,
 361:             min_risk_reward=self.min_rr,
 362:             min_lot_fallback=self.min_lot_fallback,
 363:             risk_mode=self.risk_mode,
 364:         )
 365: 
 366: 
 254:     """The stricter value of each governed field. The result keeps the cage's class (a
 255:     :class:`HighRiskCage` stays one, with its own bounds). An AGGRESSIVE profile against a standard cage
 256:     raises :class:`HighRiskRefused`: it is never clamped into a standard cage's ceiling (PLAN Â§A36)."""
 257:     if profile is not None and profile.aggressive and not isinstance(local, HighRiskCage):
 258:         raise HighRiskRefused("an aggressive profile needs the high-risk cage")
 259:     if profile is None:
 260:         return local
 261:     daily = _lower(local.max_daily_loss_percent, profile.max_daily_loss_percent)
 262:     total = _lower(local.max_total_open_risk_percent, profile.total_open_risk_percent)
 263:     per_trade = min(_lower(local.max_risk_per_trade_percent, profile.risk_per_trade_percent), daily, total)
 264:     positions = local.max_open_positions
 265:     if profile.max_open_positions is not None:
 266:         positions = min(positions, profile.max_open_positions)
 267:     min_rr = (
 268:         local.min_risk_reward
 269:         if profile.min_risk_reward is None
 270:         else max(local.min_risk_reward, profile.min_risk_reward)
 271:     )
 272:     update: dict[str, float | int | bool] = {
 273:         "min_lot_fallback": local.min_lot_fallback and bool(profile.min_lot_fallback),
 274:         "max_daily_loss_percent": daily,
 275:         "max_total_open_risk_percent": total,
 276:         "max_risk_per_trade_percent": per_trade,
 277:         "max_open_positions": positions,
 278:         "min_risk_reward": min_rr,
 279:     }
 280:     # validate the combination (bounds and coherence) exactly as a local config would be
 281:     return type(local).model_validate({**local.model_dump(), **update})

 107: def run_until(d: Demo, event: EventType, limit: int = 80) -> None:
 108:     for _ in range(limit):
 109:         d.engine.cycle()
 110:         if event in d.types():
 111:             return
 112:         d.clock.advance(30)
 113:     raise AssertionError(f"{event} never happened; events: {d.types()}")

    def test_a_non_finite_mean_gives_no_lean(self, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch):

139:     def test_a_non_finite_mean_gives_no_lean(self, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch):
        frames, at = h1_frame([100.0 + i for i in range(NEEDED)])
        h1 = frames[Timeframe.H1]
        # an infinite CLOSE inside the last 50 H4 buckets (bucket 60 = H1 bars 240-243): inf survives the
        # dropna, unlike NaN, and poisons the 50-bar mean. Bucket 10 would not - it is outside the window.
        h1.iloc[243, h1.columns.get_loc("close")] = float("inf")
        assert edge_lean(frames, at, Timeframe.M5) is None

    def test_a_non_finite_mean_gives_no_lean(self, monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch):

176: def test_a_tiny_atr_overflows_to_infinity_and_is_refused(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch):
    """The developer session was right and I was wrong (2026-10-27).

    I wrote that the final `isfinite` guard was unreachable, because the mean and width guards stop every
    non-finite input. They do - but a FINITE, positive, tiny ATR (1e-320) passes both and then overflows
    the division to infinity. `inf < 0` is False, so a gate written as "refuse when the lean is negative"
    would open on no evidence at all. The guard is live code, and this is the input that reaches it.

def test_a_tiny_atr_overflows_to_infinity_and_is_refused(monkeypatch: pytest.MonkeyPatch):
    """The developer session was right and I was wrong (2026-10-27).

179:     I wrote that the final `isfinite` guard was unreachable, because the mean and width guards stop every
    non-finite input. They do - but a FINITE, positive, tiny ATR (1e-320) passes both and then overflows
    the division to infinity. `inf < 0` is False, so a gate written as "refuse when the lean is negative"
    would open on no evidence at all. The guard is live code, and this is the input that reaches it.
    """
    frames, at = h1_frame([100.0 + i for i in range(NEEDED)])
    monkeypatch.setattr(module, "atr", lambda *_a, **_k: pd.Series([1e-320] * 200))

   3: "make it profitable first - no before / after the exam").
   4: 
   5: Plain code, no AI, so it can be tuned fast. Each bot watches for its OWN structure at every M5 close inside the
   6: owner's windows (Asia 07:00-09:00, London 13:00-15:00 Thai, New York 08:00-11:30 NY) and holds its own position;
   7: a trend bot and the range bot can overlap, the two trend bots cannot (their conditions are opposites).
   8: 
   9: - DOWN: MetaTrader 5's ZigZag (12, 5, 3) on the H1 bars closed by then shows a lower high AND a lower low; its
  10:   last leg A (high) -> B (low); price pulled back RETRACE of it; an M5 box (6-24 bars, at most BOX of the leg
  11:   tall) - the quiet; an M5 close under the box = SELL; stop over the box by BUFFER of the box height;
  12:   take-profit at B, at least MIN_TP USD and MIN_R x the risk. UP: the mirror;
  13: - RANGE: the box comes from the BIG picture (owner 2026-10-26: "sideways has to be measured on H1-H4"):
  14:   ``range_tf`` names the frame whose ZigZag gives the box - H4, H1, or 'both' (the H4 box, and the H1 swings
  15:   must sit inside it). That frame must not be stepping (no lower-high-and-lower-low, no higher pair), and price
  16:   must be inside the box. Then an M5 bar over the box top closing back under it = SELL (a sweep closed back),
  17:   stop over that bar's high + BUFFER x its range, take-profit TP_RANGE of the way to the box low; the mirror at
  18:   the bottom. ``range_mode`` 'opposite' takes the other side, 'break' trades an M5 close out of the box;
  19: - shared by the account: no entry into a push still running toward the stop (the 60 minutes before ran more than
  20:   RUN x the stop distance), and the windows;
  21: - the exit, ``exit_mode``: 'tp' the stop and take-profit alone; 'be' the stop to break-even once the trade is
  22:   BE_AT x risk ahead; 'trail' the same, then the stop follows the extreme of the last TRAIL_MIN minutes of M1
  23:   bars; 'struct' out when STRUCT_BARS M5 bars close back across the entry. 'tp' leans on where the test stops
  24:   (the Thai day's end carried 29-48 % of each bot's gross R), so it is a baseline, not a design;

  54:     box: float = 0.25  # the box at most this share of the H1 leg tall
  55:     buffer: float = 0.2  # the stop past the box by this share of the box height
  56:     min_tp: float = 0.0  # the 5 USD floor, off since 2026-10-27 (it rejected 1 of 496 signals)

  60:     spread: float = 0.30

  63:     trail_min: int = 30  # 'trail': the stop follows the extreme of the last this many minutes of M1 bars
  64:     struct_bars: int = 3  # 'struct': this many M5 closes back across the entry end the trade
  65:     max_hours: float = (
  66:         0  # the trade is closed at this bar's close after this many hours (0: at the day's end)
  67:     )
  68:     bots: tuple[str, ...] = ("DOWN", "UP", "RANGE")

  70:     range_mode: str = "fade"  # fade: sell a sweep closed back; opposite: the same trigger, the other side;
  71:     # break: an M5 close out of the box, traded with the break
  72:     range_tf: str = "H4"  # where the box comes from: "H4", "H1" or "both" (the H4 box with H1 inside it)
  73:     range_flat: bool = True  # the box frame must not be stepping up or down

  88: 
  89: # The configuration the app actually ships (app/strategy/trend_box.py, range_break.py and the three_bots
  90: # lane of config.yaml). It is a dict because the three bots do NOT share one P: RANGE differs from the
  91: # others in KIND - "break" is an M5 close out of the box, while P()'s default "fade" sells a sweep that
  92: # closed back inside, a different setup entirely.
  93: #
  94: # On 2026-10-27 a research script measured P() instead of this and produced an afternoon of conclusions
  95: # about a setup we do not run (the shipped bots are +0.102 R a trade; the defaults are -0.106). `run`
  96: # now refuses the bare defaults so that cannot happen silently again.
  97: SHIPPED: dict[str, "P"] = {}  # filled below, once P exists

 100: def _shipped() -> dict[str, "P"]:
 101:     return {
 102:         "DOWN": replace(P(), bots=("DOWN",), box=0.2, buffer=0.2, min_r=1.2),
 103:         "UP": replace(P(), bots=("UP",), box=0.33, buffer=0.2, min_r=1.2),
 104:         "RANGE": replace(
 105:             P(), bots=("RANGE",), range_tf="H4", range_mode="break", tp_range=0.5, buffer=0.3, min_r=0.8
 106:         ),  # fmt: skip

 113: def windows(day: pd.Timestamp) -> list[tuple[pd.Timestamp, pd.Timestamp]]:
 114:     th, ny = pd.Timestamp(day.date(), tz=TH), pd.Timestamp(day.date(), tz=NY)
 115:     return [
 116:         ((th + pd.Timedelta(hours=7)).tz_convert("UTC"), (th + pd.Timedelta(hours=9)).tz_convert("UTC")),
 117:         ((th + pd.Timedelta(hours=13)).tz_convert("UTC"), (th + pd.Timedelta(hours=15)).tz_convert("UTC")),
 118:         (
 119:             (ny + pd.Timedelta(hours=8)).tz_convert("UTC"),
 120:             (ny + pd.Timedelta(hours=11, minutes=30)).tz_convert("UTC"),
 121:         ),
 122:     ]

 125: def market(h1: pd.DataFrame) -> tuple[str, list]:  # the census only: the bots no longer switch on it
 126:     sw, _ = zigzag(h1.high.to_numpy(float), h1.low.to_numpy(float))
 127:     highs = [s for s in sw if s.kind == 1]
 128:     lows = [s for s in sw if s.kind == -1]
 129:     if len(highs) < 2 or len(lows) < 2:
 130:         return "NONE", sw
 131:     if highs[-1].price < highs[-2].price and lows[-1].price < lows[-2].price:
 132:         return "DOWN", sw
 133:     if highs[-1].price > highs[-2].price and lows[-1].price > lows[-2].price:
 134:         return "UP", sw
 135:     return "RANGE", sw

 137: 
 138: def settle(m1: pd.DataFrame, t: Trade, until: pd.Timestamp, p: P) -> Trade:
 139:     """Walk the M1 bars from the entry to ``until``: the stop (moved by ``exit_mode``) and the take-profit, the
 140:     worse of the two first when a bar holds both; else the last close. The spread is taken off once."""
 141:     if p.max_hours:
 142:         until = min(until, t.at + pd.Timedelta(hours=p.max_hours))
 143:     x = m1[(m1.index >= t.at) & (m1.index < until)]
 144:     sell = t.side == "SELL"
 145:     risk = abs(t.stop - t.entry)
 146:     hi = x.high.to_numpy()
 147:     lo = x.low.to_numpy()
 148:     cl = x.close.to_numpy()
 149:     ix = x.index
 150:     stop, best = t.stop, t.entry  # best: the furthest price reached in the trade's favour
 151:     exit_px, out, when = None, ("time" if p.max_hours else "eod"), until
 152:     back = 0  # 'struct': M5 closes in a row back across the entry
 153:     for i in range(len(x)):
 154:         if (hi[i] >= stop) if sell else (lo[i] <= stop):
 155:             exit_px, out, when = stop, ("SL" if stop == t.stop else "trail"), ix[i]
 156:             break
 157:         if (lo[i] <= t.tp) if sell else (hi[i] >= t.tp):
 158:             exit_px, out, when = t.tp, "TP", ix[i]
 159:             break
 160:         best = min(best, lo[i]) if sell else max(best, hi[i])
 161:         ahead = (t.entry - best) if sell else (best - t.entry)
 162:         if p.exit_mode in ("be", "trail") and ahead >= p.be_at * risk:
 163:             moved = t.entry
 164:             if p.exit_mode == "trail" and i >= 1:
 165:                 j = max(0, i - p.trail_min + 1)
 166:                 moved = float(hi[j : i + 1].max()) if sell else float(lo[j : i + 1].min())
 167:             stop = min(stop, max(moved, t.entry)) if sell else max(stop, min(moved, t.entry))
 168:         if p.exit_mode == "struct" and (i + 1) % 5 == 0:
 169:             back = back + 1 if ((cl[i] > t.entry) if sell else (cl[i] < t.entry)) else 0
 170:             if back >= p.struct_bars:
 171:                 exit_px, out, when = float(cl[i]), "struct", ix[i]
 172:                 break
 173:     if exit_px is None:
 174:         exit_px = float(cl[-1]) if len(x) else t.entry
 175:     gain = (t.entry - exit_px) if sell else (exit_px - t.entry)
 176:     t.r, t.out = (gain - p.spread) / risk, out
 177:     t.extra["closed"] = when
 178:     return t
 179: 
 180: 
 181: def run(
 182:     f: dict, params: P | dict[str, P], start: pd.Timestamp = START, end: pd.Timestamp = END
 183: ) -> list[Trade]:
 184:     """Every bot of ``params`` (one P for all its bots, or one P per bot) side by side, each with its own
 185:     position, so a trend trade and a range trade can be open at once."""
 186:     if isinstance(params, dict):
 187:         bare = [b for b, v in params.items() if v == P()]
 188:         if bare:
 189:             raise ValueError(
 190:                 f"run() was given the bare P() defaults for {bare}. Pass bots.SHIPPED, or "
 191:                 "replace(P(), ...) when the variation is deliberate."
 192:             )
 193:     if isinstance(params, P) and params == P():
 194:         raise ValueError(
 195:             "run() was given the bare P() defaults. Those are not the configuration we ship - RANGE's "
 196:             "default range_mode is 'fade', while the shipped bot is 'break'. Pass bots.SHIPPED (a dict, "
 197:             "one P per bot), or replace(P(), ...) when you mean to vary something deliberately. "
 198:             "(The trap that cost 2026-10-27's afternoon.)"
 199:         )
 200:     per: dict[str, P] = params if isinstance(params, dict) else {b: params for b in params.bots}
 201:     m1, h1 = f[Timeframe.M1], f[Timeframe.H1]
 202:     h4 = h1.resample("4h").agg({"open": "first", "high": "max", "low": "min", "close": "last"}).dropna()
 203:     m1 = m1[(m1.index >= start - pd.Timedelta(days=3)) & (m1.index < end + pd.Timedelta(days=2))]
 204:     m5 = m1.resample("5min").agg({"open": "first", "high": "max", "low": "min", "close": "last"}).dropna()
 205:     trades: list[Trade] = []
 206:     busy = dict.fromkeys(per, start)  # each bot's own position: free again from this time
 207:     for day in pd.date_range(start, end, freq="D"):
 208:         if day.weekday() >= 5:
 209:             continue
 210:         day_end = (pd.Timestamp(day.date(), tz=TH) + pd.Timedelta(days=1, hours=7)).tz_convert("UTC")
 211:         for w0, w1 in windows(day):
 212:             bars = m5[(m5.index >= w0 - pd.Timedelta(hours=2)) & (m5.index < w1)]
 213:             seen, sw, sw4 = None, [], []
 214:             for i in range(len(bars)):
 215:                 t_close = bars.index[i] + pd.Timedelta(minutes=5)
 216:                 if t_close <= w0 or t_close > w1:
 217:                     continue
 218:                 hx = h1[h1.index + pd.Timedelta(hours=1) <= t_close].iloc[-400:]
 219:                 if seen != len(hx):
 220:                     seen = len(hx)
 221:                     sw, _ = zigzag(hx.high.to_numpy(float), hx.low.to_numpy(float))
 222:                     h4x = h4[h4.index + pd.Timedelta(hours=4) <= t_close].iloc[-300:]
 223:                     sw4, _ = zigzag(h4x.high.to_numpy(float), h4x.low.to_numpy(float))
 224:                 if len(sw) < 3:
 225:                     continue
 226:                 past = bars.iloc[: i + 1]
 227:                 for bot, p in per.items():
 228:                     if t_close < busy[bot]:
 229:                         continue  # this bot's own trade is still open
 230:                     tr = signal(bot, sw, sw4, past, p, t_close)
 231:                     if tr is None:
 232:                         continue
 233:                     before = m1[(m1.index >= t_close - pd.Timedelta(minutes=60)) & (m1.index < t_close)]
 234:                     runup = (
 235:                         (tr.entry - before.low.min()) if tr.side == "SELL" else (before.high.max() - tr.entry)
 236:                     )
 237:                     if p.run and runup > p.run * abs(tr.stop - tr.entry):
 238:                         continue
 239:                     tr = settle(m1, tr, day_end, p)
 240:                     trades.append(tr)
 241:                     busy[bot] = tr.extra["closed"]
 242:     return sorted(trades, key=lambda x: x.at)
 243: 
 244: 
 245: def stepping(sws: list) -> str:
 246:     """'DOWN' (lower high and lower low), 'UP', or 'FLAT' - from the last two confirmed swings of each kind."""
 247:     highs = [s for s in sws if s.kind == 1]
 248:     lows = [s for s in sws if s.kind == -1]
 249:     if len(highs) < 2 or len(lows) < 2:
 250:         return "FLAT"
 251:     if highs[-1].price < highs[-2].price and lows[-1].price < lows[-2].price:
 252:         return "DOWN"
 253:     if highs[-1].price > highs[-2].price and lows[-1].price > lows[-2].price:
 254:         return "UP"
 255:     return "FLAT"
 256: 
 257: 
 258: def range_box(sw: list, sw4: list, p: P) -> tuple[float, float] | None:
 259:     """(top, bottom) of the big-picture box, or None when that frame is not sideways."""
 260:     big = sw4 if p.range_tf in ("H4", "both") else sw
 261:     if len(big) < 2:
 262:         return None
 263:     if p.range_flat and stepping(big) != "FLAT":
 264:         return None
 265:     highs = [s.price for s in big if s.kind == 1]
 266:     lows = [s.price for s in big if s.kind == -1]
 267:     if not highs or not lows:
 268:         return None
 269:     top, floor = highs[-1], lows[-1]
 270:     if top <= floor:
 271:         return None
 272:     if p.range_tf == "both":  # the H1 swings have to sit inside the H4 box
 273:         h1h = [s.price for s in sw if s.kind == 1][-1:]
 274:         h1l = [s.price for s in sw if s.kind == -1][-1:]
 275:         if not h1h or not h1l or h1h[0] > top or h1l[0] < floor:
 276:             return None
 277:     return top, floor
 278: 
 279: 
 280: def signal(bot: str, sw: list, sw4: list, past: pd.DataFrame, p: P, t: pd.Timestamp) -> Trade | None:
 281:     """The bot's own setup on these bars, or None. Each bot tests its own structure, so a down leg and a range
 282:     can hold at the same moment; the two trend bots cannot, their conditions being opposites."""
 283:     price = float(past.close.iloc[-1])
 284:     highs = [s for s in sw if s.kind == 1]
 285:     lows = [s for s in sw if s.kind == -1]
 286:     if len(highs) < 2 or len(lows) < 2:
 287:         return None
 288:     if bot in ("DOWN", "UP"):
 289:         down = bot == "DOWN"
 290:         stepping = (
 291:             (highs[-1].price < highs[-2].price and lows[-1].price < lows[-2].price)
 292:             if down
 293:             else (highs[-1].price > highs[-2].price and lows[-1].price > lows[-2].price)
 294:         )
 295:         if not stepping:
 296:             return None
 297:         a, b = sw[-2], sw[-1]  # the last confirmed leg
 298:         if (a.kind, b.kind) != ((1, -1) if down else (-1, 1)):
 299:             return None
 300:         leg = abs(a.price - b.price)
 301:         if leg <= 0:
 302:             return None
 303:         for n in range(24, 5, -1):  # the longest box that still fits
 304:             if len(past) < n + 1:
 305:                 continue
 306:             box = past.iloc[-n - 1 : -1]
 307:             top, floor = float(box.high.max()), float(box.low.min())
 308:             if top - floor > p.box * leg:
 309:                 continue
 310:             mid = (top + floor) / 2
 311:             retr = (mid - b.price) / leg if down else (b.price - mid) / leg
 312:             if not (p.retrace[0] <= retr <= p.retrace[1]):
 313:                 return None
 314:             if down and price < floor:
 315:                 stop, tp = top + p.buffer * (top - floor), b.price
 316:             elif not down and price > top:
 317:                 stop, tp = floor - p.buffer * (top - floor), b.price
 318:             else:
 319:                 return None
 320:             risk, reward = abs(stop - price), abs(price - tp)
 321:             if reward < p.min_tp or reward < p.min_r * risk or (tp >= price if down else tp <= price):
 322:                 return None
 323:             side = "SELL" if down else "BUY"
 324:             return Trade(bot, side, t, price, stop, tp, extra={"retrace": round(retr, 2), "box": n})
 325:         return None
 326:     box = range_box(sw, sw4, p)
 327:     if box is None:
 328:         return None
 329:     hh, ll = box
 330:     bar = past.iloc[-1]
 331:     rng = hh - ll
 332:     if p.range_mode == "break":  # price leaves the box: the close through decides, no 'inside' gate
 333:         prev = float(past.close.iloc[-2]) if len(past) > 1 else price
 334:         if price > hh >= prev:
 335:             stop, tp, side = hh - p.buffer * rng * 0.25, price + p.tp_range * rng, "BUY"
 336:         elif price < ll <= prev:
 337:             stop, tp, side = ll + p.buffer * rng * 0.25, price - p.tp_range * rng, "SELL"
 338:         else:
 339:             return None
 340:         risk, reward = abs(stop - price), abs(tp - price)
 341:         if risk <= 0 or reward < p.min_tp or reward < p.min_r * risk:
 342:             return None
 343:         return Trade("RANGE", side, t, price, stop, tp, extra={"range": round(rng, 1)})
 344:     if not ll < price < hh:  # a fade is taken from inside the box only
 345:         return None
 346:     if bar.high > hh and bar.close < hh:
 347:         stop = float(bar.high) + p.buffer * float(bar.high - bar.low)
 348:         tp = price - p.tp_range * (price - ll)
 349:         side = "SELL"
 350:     elif bar.low < ll and bar.close > ll:
 351:         stop = float(bar.low) - p.buffer * float(bar.high - bar.low)
 352:         tp = price + p.tp_range * (hh - price)
 353:         side = "BUY"
 354:     else:
 355:         return None
 356:     if p.range_mode == "opposite":  # the same moment, the other side: stop and take-profit mirrored
 357:         side = "BUY" if side == "SELL" else "SELL"
 358:         stop, tp = 2 * price - stop, 2 * price - tp
 359:     risk, reward = abs(stop - price), abs(tp - price)
 360:     if reward < p.min_tp or reward < p.min_r * risk:
 361:         return None
 362:     return Trade("RANGE", side, t, price, stop, tp, extra={"range": round(rng, 1)})
 363: 
 364: 
 365: def report(trades: list[Trade], title: str) -> None:
 366:     df = pd.DataFrame([{"bot": t.bot, "side": t.side, "at": t.at, "r": t.r, "out": t.out} for t in trades])
 367:     print(f"\n== {title}: {len(df)} trades ==")
 368:     if not len(df):
 369:         return
 370:     print("| bot | trades | win % | R / trade (after spread) | total R | weeks + / - | worst streak |")
 371:     print("|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|")
 372:     for bot, g in [*df.groupby("bot"), ("ALL", df)]:
 373:         wk = g.groupby(g["at"].dt.tz_convert(TH).dt.to_period("W")).r.sum()
 374:         streak = cur = 0
 375:         for r in g.r:
 376:             cur = cur + 1 if r < 0 else 0
 377:             streak = max(streak, cur)
 378:         print(
 379:             f"| {bot} | {len(g)} | {(g.r > 0).mean():.0%} | {g.r.mean():+.3f} | {g.r.sum():+.1f} | "
 380:             f"{(wk > 0).sum()} / {(wk <= 0).sum()} | {streak} |"
 381:         )
 382: 
 383: 
 384: def main() -> None:
 385:     sys.stdout.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8")  # type: ignore[attr-defined]
 386:     hs = ParquetHistoryStore(ROOT / "data/history")
 387:     f = {t: to_frame(hs.load("FBS-Demo", "XAUUSD", t, None, None)) for t in (Timeframe.M1, Timeframe.H1)}
 388:     report(run(f, SHIPPED), "the three bots as shipped, 2025-10-20 .. 2026-10-23")
 389: 
 390: 
 391: if __name__ == "__main__":
 392:     main()

codex
# Audit report: 02cbebf and 82d3f1f

**Verdict: the code adds a fail-closed plan gate and an H4 edge gate for the three bots, but the running DEMO engine has not loaded those changes. The edge rule also does not cover the enabled PLAN-1 strategies.** The requested test suite could not start in this read-only environment, so the code is not fully verified.

## 1. Do the commit messages tell the truth?

**02cbebf:** Its code claims check out against the diff: `DayPlan` treats missing, empty, unreadable, or invalid UTF-8 files as unplanned; the mode gate blocks new orders; and the H4 rule rejects unreadable or negative leans for the three bot strategies. The recorded `+0.102 → +0.210 R` and `+0.214 / +0.207` figures match the new rule set’s tables. I did not independently reproduce those figures from the historical data.

The RR scope claim also checks out: the global `risk.min_risk_reward` remains **1.0** in [config.yaml](/C:/Users/korap/taa/config.yaml:253); the **0.8** value is on the `three_bots` lane at [config.yaml](/C:/Users/korap/taa/config.yaml:226). The profile’s lower RR is combined conservatively with the local cage in [limits.py](/C:/Users/korap/taa/app/risk/limits.py:267), then the lane’s own value is applied in [decision_engine.py](/C:/Users/korap/taa/app/engine/decision_engine.py:257). I found no global RR cage reduction or plan1 RR loosening in the diff.

**82d3f1f:** The replay guard exists in `day.py`, and review rounds 2–5 are present. But the statement in `RULES_LOCKED.md` that the earlier mistakes are “now impossible to repeat” overstates the guard: [test_bots.py](/C:/Users/korap/taa/research/2026-10-26-three-bots/test_bots.py:30) explicitly allows deliberate parameter variations. The tests protect against bare defaults and compare the shipped values to app defaults; they cannot prevent a future script from supplying a different, incorrect configuration.

There is also a stale statement in [HANDOFF.md](/C:/Users/korap/taa/docs/HANDOFF.md:425) claiming the final `isfinite` guard is unreachable. The code and a dedicated test show otherwise: a finite ATR of `1e-320` can overflow the division, and the guard refuses it at [edge_side.py](/C:/Users/korap/taa/app/strategy/edge_side.py:81) and [test_strategy_edge_side.py](/C:/Users/korap/taa/tests/unit/test_strategy_edge_side.py:176).

## 2. Standing rules against the finished code

**P26, the morning plan:** The implementation is a real pre-send gate. [orchestrator.py](/C:/Users/korap/taa/app/engine/orchestrator.py:654) reevaluates it before orders, and the DEMO integration tests cover zero `order_send` calls without a plan and orders flowing after a plan is restored. Missing, unreadable, or invalid text fails closed.

There is a completeness gap: [day_plan.py](/C:/Users/korap/taa/app/risk/day_plan.py:51) accepts any non-whitespace UTF-8 text as a plan. It does not check for the day’s condition, levels, trend, edge side, or scenarios. The gate therefore refuses an empty file, but a one-character file opens it.

More urgently, [HANDOFF.md](/C:/Users/korap/taa/docs/HANDOFF.md:408) says the DEMO process was not restarted after the H4 gate was switched on, and lines 423–424 say P26 was still only a note. The running process therefore does not have the new gates, regardless of the current source code.

**Larger-timeframe edge, unreadable means refuse:** `trend_box` and `range_break` refuse when the H4 lean is unavailable or against the trade, when `require_edge_side` is enabled. But that setting is enabled only for the three new bots in [config.yaml](/C:/Users/korap/taa/config.yaml:211). The four enabled PLAN-1 strategies at [config.yaml](/C:/Users/korap/taa/config.yaml:159) do not apply an edge-side gate. Since the standing rule says “every trade,” this is a rule violation: PLAN-1 entries can still trade without a larger-timeframe edge reading. The AI strategy is disabled in this config.

**Stops and kill switch:** I found no diff change that weakens the trade stop or kill switch. `min_stop_atr` is new but defaults to zero and is not enabled in the shipped config. The loss breakers are already deactivated in the baseline, consistent with the owner’s decision. That leaves the documented DEMO exposure—7.5% risk per trade, up to two positions, about 15% combined open risk—with stops and the kill switch as protection. A missing gate can allow an entry the owner’s edge rule would reject; if it loses, the stop can lose up to the trade’s risk budget, and two positions can put roughly 15% of equity at risk before gaps or execution effects. There is no active loss breaker to stop a sequence of such losses.

**Frozen rule set 2:** The diff appends rule set 2 and preserves rule set 1 marked withdrawn. I found no subsequent movement of set 2’s threshold or reported figures in the supplied diff. The H4 threshold is still zero. The document itself acknowledges the figures were selected after seeing both historical halves, so the forward test remains necessary.

## 3. Changes with broader impact than their purpose

The earlier global `min_risk_reward` cage reduction is **not present**. The global value remains 1.0; 0.8 is scoped to the `three_bots` lane, and the profile combination remains conservative for lanes without their own override.

The config does enable all three bot strategies where they were disabled. That is within the stated purpose of running the three-bot system, but it is a consequential change: it adds active strategies on the DEMO account, and the handoff says the running process has not loaded the new edge gate.

I found no unintended change to stop placement or kill-switch behavior. The profile schema now accepts RR values below 1.0, but the effective local cage still prevents that value from lowering the global floor for lanes without an explicit lane RR. Plan1 remains at the 1.0 cage.

## 4. Dead code, reachability, and comments

The handoff’s “unreachable” `isfinite` statement is contradicted by live code and the overflow test described above.

`app/strategy/weighing.py` is an inert helper with no production call site in the diff. It has no live trading effect. The new `edge_side()` convenience function also has no production caller; production strategy checks use `edge_lean()` and `behind()` directly. Neither appears to cause an order-safety defect.

The P26 tests that normally create a plan in their fixtures would not alone prove refusal, but the added dedicated DEMO tests explicitly delete the plan and assert zero broker sends, then restore it and assert orders flow. That contract is covered in source.

## 5. Requested checks

- `.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q`: **could not collect or run tests**. Pytest exited before collection because it could not find a writable temporary directory. No suite pass/fail count is available; this was not a green run.
- `ruff check app tests`: **passed** (“All checks passed!”).
- `mypy app`: **did not complete**. Mypy 2.4.0 exited with an internal error; with `--no-incremental --show-traceback`, the cause was `sqlite3.OperationalError: unable to open database file`, consistent with the read-only environment.
- `bandit -q -r app -c pyproject.toml`: **passed**, exit code 0. It emitted warnings about words in comments being interpreted as test names.

## Fix before the next trading day

The most urgent issue is operational: the current DEMO process is trading without the code changes’ P26 and H4 gates. Stop relying on that process until the engine-verified backtest is complete and the updated engine has been restarted with a valid day plan. Before treating the edge rule as satisfied, extend the larger-timeframe edge check to the enabled PLAN-1 strategies too.

**The engine should not keep running overnight as it is.** The report is based on the committed diff and handoff; I made no file changes.
